<a id="rate-equation-flux"></a>

## 23 November 2026 · Reaction direction and dissipation in the deterministic representation

For `cells.rate_equations@1`, the driving free energy is the stoichiometric change in chemical potential minus reservoir work, in units of k_BT. An enabled reaction with positive forward factor runs forward when this quantity is negative, reverses when it is positive and stalls at zero. A disabled reaction or a zero forward factor gives zero flux at every force. Lean proves nonnegative instantaneous dissipation for each reaction and for any finite sum of them. The proof reuses the ATP-cycle direction lemmas and Mathlib’s finite sum of nonnegative terms. Time integration and floating-point accuracy have their own comparisons. [Lean proof](CellsProofs/Chemistry/RateEquations.lean) · [Engine flux](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/ddcf32c1050c6bda14e476b0ec22b7defa010db3/src/cells/models/assembly_lineages_v2/rate_equations.py#L614)

In a compiled CPU float64 `cells.assembly_lineages@2` world, the two reactions A ⇌ B ⇌ C matched independent linear rates at five states within 1.10 × 10⁻¹¹ reaction events/s. Both stalled at equal counts, reversed across relative concentration changes of 10⁻⁹ and dissipated positive energy on both sides. Two unused slots returned zero. With A held at 2 mM and B at 4 mM, the engine gave ΔF = +0.69315 k_BT. Omitting the reservoir work gives −5.52146 k_BT and predicts the opposite direction. At equal 2 mM concentrations the residual force was −8.88 × 10⁻¹⁶ k_BT. [Compiled comparison](checks/rate_equation_flux.py)

Eicher’s two extract courses started at F6P/G6P ratios of 5.168 and 0.0464. F6P fell by 3.787 mM in the first tube and rose by 1.172 mM in the second. The initial Cells flux had the corresponding sign in both cases when the ideal pair used the authors’ fitted equilibrium ratio of 0.286. This is an in-sample comparison of direction. The readings give changes over intervals, with no per-point uncertainty or calorimetry. [Eicher et al., Fig. 5](https://www.mdpi.com/2218-1989/2/4/818) · [Worksheet cells and measurements](checks/data/pgi_flux_direction.json)

In a separate rabbit-muscle preparation at 30 °C and pH 8.5, kinetic parameters gave F6P/G6P = 0.32. With that as its input, the compiled reaction changed direction at 0.320. The independently measured equilibrium ratio in the same preparation was 0.30. The difference is +0.020. Table I gives no uncertainty for that endpoint, so the magnitude has no quantitative agreement verdict. The comparison uses each preparation’s own parameters. [Dyson and Noltmann, Table I and Fig. 13](https://doi.org/10.1016/S0021-9258(18)93559-3) · [Measurement record](checks/data/pgi_flux_direction.json)

The run `lean-rate-equation-flux-20261123` records the frozen source, `rate-equation-flux.json`, `measurement-audit.json`, `axioms.log` and the verification results. Regenerate the numerical report with `python -B checks/rate_equation_flux.py` in the Cells CPU environment from the Lean README.

<a id="forms-face-carriage"></a>

## 23 November 2026 · A moving binder carries its ligands

For each stored species, the face sums each mover’s signed transfer multiplied by the number of that species it carries. A binder form counts once toward the binder and contributes its ligand counts relative to the reference form. Lean proves that this projection conserves every row across the closed network and recovers the charge of the movers. It also proves that at zero voltage two forms with opposing concentration gradients can exchange at the same diffusivity with zero net binder flux and positive occupied-form flux. This uses the existing log-mean law, including its small-gradient branch. [Formal results](CellsProofs/Transport/FormsFace.lean) · [Mover table](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/7ece26559d7b459928d6a7a7b819fb613b51a984/src/cells/laws/routes/logmean_electrodiffusion_forms.py#L141)

On Cells `7ece2655`, a three-node kernel control moved 8 and −4 fmol/s of ligand on two adjoining faces at zero net binder transfer. The mobile form carried two ligands relative to the base. A second control used a count of −1 and gave −4 and 2 fmol/s. Omitting the ligand from the row booking preserved both global totals but missed the middle node by 12 and 6 fmol/s respectively. Immobilizing the binder stopped transport in these controls, which had no direct free-ligand route. [Replay driver](checks/forms_face.py) · [Stored-row projection](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/7ece26559d7b459928d6a7a7b819fb613b51a984/src/cells/numerics/surface_transport.py#L131)

A separate CPU float64 run through `resolved_pump_tissue@3` used the declared two-lumen binding fixture and four 1 ms IMEX steps. By 4 ms the face had transferred 0.0439183230 fmol of binder. Bound protons accounted for 0.00446872212 fmol and free protons for 0.0000000721629 fmol. The largest difference between an independently reconstructed binder or proton transfer and either node’s recorded inventory change was 6.94 × 10⁻¹⁸ fmol across the five saved times. The run directory `lean-forms-face-balance-20261123` contains `forms-face.json`, `native-tests.log`, the axiom audit and a frozen copy of the driver. [Tissue fixture](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/7ece26559d7b459928d6a7a7b819fb613b51a984/tests/unit/test_rapid_binding_world.py#L685) · [Model ledger](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/7ece26559d7b459928d6a7a7b819fb613b51a984/src/cells/models/surface_pump_v3.py#L3695)

Two published experiments support carrier-assisted transport at different scales. In rabbit ventricular myocytes, a 20 nM rise in free H⁺ took 25.1 ± 5.1 s to travel between regions about 40 µm apart in HEPES and 5.93 ± 2.96 s with carbonic buffer. With carbonic anhydrase inhibited the delay was 15.53 ± 2.65 s (means ± SEM, 10, 7 and 8 cells respectively). In a dialysis experiment, adding uncrosslinked myoglobin to a polymer film raised initial oxygen flux to about three times the carrier-free value of 3 × 10⁻⁷ kg m⁻² h⁻¹. The protein subsequently leached, and crosslinking also impaired oxygen unloading. A quantitative test of the Cells face would require form-resolved transfer and both endpoint inventories under matched conditions. [Spitzer et al., Figs. 1–2](https://doi.org/10.1113/jphysiol.2001.013268) · [Figueiredo et al., Figs. 6–7](https://doi.org/10.1590/0104-6632.20140313s00002307) · [Measurement records](checks/data/mobile_buffer_transport.json)

<a id="fr38-extracellular-producer"></a>

## 23 November 2026 · A producer’s RNA inside and outside after replacement

The development fixture below passed 14 checks. The independent registered FR-38 comparison subsequently **failed R1**, including the blank and labelled route-1 readouts, and found **R2 inconclusive** on the observed convergence order. The subsequent non-blind diagnosis traced route 1 to the engine’s gross cohort loss and the reference’s net loss after blank subtraction. The engine reproduced the reference’s separate gross closed form to 2 × 10⁻¹⁴. The registered verdict stands. [Published verdict D313](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/81a98ca025ee662a0846aa13868dfc93fa87ea22/docs/DECISIONS.md#L9867) · [Route-1 diagnosis D316](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/a8f9810c791986a25a38f8ecb718cbfdf7479fec/docs/DECISIONS.md#L9972)

For one producer label, each gross export carries the cell’s source share and each return carries the outside source share. Lean proves that adding the two balances cancels both transfers. Degradation on either side and material removed with the departing cell remain in the account. A second proof bounds the original producer’s RNA in the new cell by cumulative gross return after replacement, provided the new cell starts with zero of that label and has no other input of it. This holds for any number of steps. [Formal balances](CellsProofs/Chemistry/ProducerReplacement.lean)

On public Cells `fd2bb88c`, the FR-38 fixture ran `resolved_pump_composite@1` and `resolved_pump_tissue@3` with templated polymerisation, scheduled replacement and neutral labels. The producer left at 3,000 s. All 14 native checks passed, including disk restart at 3,600 s. The independent reader recovered all 15 removed label amounts and nine counter offsets from the checkpoint. The combined durable RNA balance closed within 6.01 × 10⁻²³ fmol. The new cell contained 1.0534 × 10⁻²⁶ fmol of founder RNA after 1.1173 × 10⁻²⁶ fmol had returned. Swapping two extracellular labels moved 8.114 × 10⁻⁹ fmol to the wrong producer and preserved total RNA. The comparison detected it. [Checkpoint reader](checks/producer_replacement.py) · [Native fixture and its labelled outputs](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/fd2bb88ce444b48344236024a2a18844f59041a4/validation/gene_regulation/replace_restart.py)

At 4,800 s the labelled stock gave 11.02171 founder copy-equivalents outside. The reconstruction from export and outside degradation gave 11.00904, within the 0.02739-copy bound. All three post-replacement outputs had finite bounds. At the deliberately sparse output times 0, 3,000 and 4,800 s, the earlier reader accepted 100 against 1.7077 with an infinite bound. The fixed reader reports insufficient resolution and a null bound. An additional observation at 1,500 s exposes the disagreement. The earlier nine-verdict intracellular fixture also passed the updated Lean-side reader. The evidence run `lean-export-retention-leak-20261123` contains the public engine snapshot, native checkpoint and output in `restart/`, and the independent results in `producer.json` and `producer-legacy.json`. [Regression cases](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/fd2bb88ce444b48344236024a2a18844f59041a4/tests/unit/test_fr38_replace_restart.py)

Alexander et al. measured about 370 donor miR-146a copies per recipient cell in lysates at 24 h. The recipient mouse dendritic cells lacked the assayed RNA gene, the donor cells were absent during exposure, and the study included a donor-null control. The count includes surface-associated RNA. A recipient reporter supported intracellular access for some RNA. This provides a source attribution and a single endpoint. Estimating gross return or post-wash persistence requires a time course measuring both compartments. [Figure 3](https://www.nature.com/articles/ncomms8321/figures/3) · [Figure 4i](https://www.nature.com/articles/ncomms8321/figures/4) · [Measurement record](checks/data/alexander2015_donor_rna.json)

The checkpoint records extracellular degradation as a physical total. Individual producer amounts are checked using the saved shares and the retention reconstruction. The reader rejected four altered copies of the report. They contained a label swap at fixed total, an infinite bound, a missing bound or an insufficient-resolution status attached to a nominal pass. These control outcomes are in `reader-controls.json`. [Public fixture](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/fd2bb88ce444b48344236024a2a18844f59041a4/validation/gene_regulation/replace_restart.py)

<a id="fr38-pre-replacement-resolution"></a>

### Output times used to estimate the export-grid error

Two positive equal-size pulses can give the same cumulative export at both ends of an unsampled interval. With positive first-order degradation the earlier pulse leaves strictly less RNA at observation, as Lean proves. Cells estimates the grid error by dropping alternate output times. The known-rate integration ends at replacement, but its resolution check looks for a removed sample all the way to the later observation. On `fd2bb88c` using output times 0, 3,000 and 4,800 s correctly yielded insufficient resolution. Adding only 3,300 and 3,600 s gave an estimate of zero grid error at 4,800 s and accepted a matching 1.707738-copy readout with a 4.85 × 10⁻¹⁴-copy bound. Both grids sampled exactly the same two points during the founder’s lifetime. Adding a sample at 1,500 s instead gave a usable comparison and rejected the deliberately wrong 100-copy readout. The standard 600 s fixture samples the export interval. The independent reader requires an interior observation there. [Native estimator](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/fd2bb88ce444b48344236024a2a18844f59041a4/validation/gene_regulation/cohort.py#L187-L199) · [Three-grid control, run with `--resolution`](checks/producer_replacement.py)

<a id="mt1b-buffered-reversal"></a>

## 23 November 2026 · Mitochondrial proton-current reversal at three pH gradients

The version-2 leak reads free protons on each side of the membrane from the general binding closure. Lean specializes the held-ligand uniqueness proof to the proton at an endpoint, then the constant-field reversal proof to the two free log concentrations. At zero voltage and on the regular Bernoulli branch the inside-minus-outside reversal voltage is (RT/F)(log h_out − log h_in). The earlier series result gives the reversal ratio on the small nonzero polynomial branch. [Buffered proof](CellsProofs/Transport/BufferedProtonLeak.lean) · [Bernoulli result](CellsProofs/Transport/ProtonLeak.lean)

The `resolved_pump_tissue@3` comparison on `fd2bb88c` prepared two buffered compartments at each published pH pair. It changed outside buffer capacity between 1 and 100 mM at fixed free pH, kept the initial charge anchors balanced and found the root of the actual clamped flux. All six reversals agreed with the independent Nernst calculation within 10⁻⁶ mV. Current changed direction on opposite sides of every root. Substituting stored proton equivalents gave a nonzero current there. [Driver](checks/ims_proton_leak.py) · [Version-2 route](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/fd2bb88ce444b48344236024a2a18844f59041a4/src/cells/laws/transport/proton_leak_v2.py)

| Matrix / bath pH | Mitoplasts | Measured reversal, mean ± SEM (mV) | Cells (mV) | Cells minus mean (mV) |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| 6 / 7 | 6 | −62.23 ± 1.92 | −58.96 | +3.27 |
| 6 / 7.5 | 3 | −91.13 ± 1.82 | −88.44 | +2.69 |
| 6.5 / 6 | 4 | +28.82 ± 6.70 | +29.48 | +0.66 |

Bertholet et al. measured fatty-acid-activated currents in mouse skeletal-muscle mitoplasts. The worksheet supplies all 13 individual voltages. The driver calculates SEM from the sample SD and uses the figure’s 24 °C Nernst reference. Each prediction falls inside its group’s descriptive two-sided 95% Student t interval for the mean. The comparison was retrospective and used no fitted parameter. A separate measured bath temperature and an absolute permeability calibration remain unresolved. Full numerical outputs and both capacity controls are in `lean-export-retention-leak-20261123/proton.json`. [Extended Data Fig. 3d](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-019-1400-3/figures/8) · [Source workbook and cell ranges](checks/data/bertholet2019_proton_reversal.json)

In voltage-clamped rat alveolar epithelial cells, reducing external buffer from 100 to 1 mM preserved the measured reversal potential and typically lowered current by 10–30%. The Cells clamp fixes local free pH and gives the same zero-voltage flux at both buffer capacities within its numerical tolerance. This study constrains the interpretation of current magnitude in a different preparation. Cortese et al. independently measured differences between mitochondrial intermembrane-space and external-medium pH. [DeCoursey and Cherny](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/8804602/) · [Cortese measurements and compartment definitions](checks/data/cortese1992_ims_ph.json)

<a id="fr38-producer-replacement"></a>

## 22 November 2026 · RNA by producer across replacement and restart

The observations below document the earlier intracellular fixture. [Public extracellular replay and repaired resolution check](#fr38-extracellular-producer)

For a producer identified by cell, generation and chain, Lean combines the hold balance with the balance of each fate. At replacement, RNA still attached and RNA still in either fate enter the departing occupant’s removal records. RNA degraded during that lifetime is a separate term. Each newcomer’s cumulative counter starts at zero relative to its birth value. Mathlib’s telescoping sum proves that any number of completed lifetimes plus the current one recover the whole counter increment. For each label the disjoint row and hold reports add. Normalized shares recover the physical amount, and a newcomer with zero pre-event share receives zero of the outgoing RNA. The calculation assumes one producer per fate row and no other flow. [Lean proof](CellsProofs/Chemistry/ProducerReplacement.lean) · [Published replacement balance, §9.16](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/0a1c1b0dc8145c9c313c02a4ed57cfe5433b2efe/docs/TEMPLATED_POLYMERISATION_LAWS.md#L1483-L1515) · [Per-label event records, §9.18](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/0a1c1b0dc8145c9c313c02a4ed57cfe5433b2efe/docs/TEMPLATED_POLYMERISATION_LAWS.md#L1773-L1787)

Public Cells `0a1c1b0d` refused templated polymerisation beside scheduled cell replacement at definition time. The same admission test passed again on public `1e72a71b` (`public-1e72a71b-refusal.log`). The gene-regulation owner’s development revision `3f9893fd6b73c2138c0a931fbe99556a042d1bf8` admits the composition. The owner’s fixture runs `resolved_pump_composite@1` and `resolved_pump_tissue@3` with templated polymerisation, scheduled replacement v1 and neutral arrival labels. It replaces the producer at 3,000 s, saves to disk at 3,600 s, and runs to 4,800 s. All nine returned verdicts passed. Every resumed history field compared bit for bit with the uninterrupted run and all three resumed readouts agreed. Of the founder’s window-marked RNA, 38.0177 equivalent copies were alive at window close (1,200 s) and 31.6306 at the chase measurement (2,400 s), a fraction of 0.831997. At replacement 29.0435 equivalent copies from that window left the cell. [Public admission guard](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/0a1c1b0dc8145c9c313c02a4ed57cfe5433b2efe/src/cells/operators/templated_polymerisation.py#L969-L979) · [Independent checkpoint comparison](checks/producer_replacement.py)

The independent comparison read the saved pre-event fate amounts and the first resumed checkpoint. All eight fate amounts by label matched the departing records exactly. Four hold entries also matched, including the base label holding 8.23718256401 × 10⁻⁷ fmol. Seven cumulative counters had matching founder totals and newcomer birth offsets. Across both generations the largest hold or fate balance residual was 1.02 × 10⁻²¹ fmol. In a control, swapping the founder’s window label with the newcomer’s label on the durable fate preserved its physical total exactly. The per-label comparison detected the 4.11026306315 × 10⁻⁸ fmol assigned to the wrong producer. [Checkpoint reader and switched-label control](checks/producer_replacement.py)

**Development report writer repaired.** On the first revision the native command wrote the checkpoint and then raised `TypeError` while encoding four NumPy booleans. The owner’s acceptance test passed because it read the verdicts in memory. At the repaired development revision `4099775c8e931c38d276a2fe8c6f3121baed60ed`, the same command wrote `replace_restart.json`, returned success and reported all nine verdicts true. The before-and-after JSON differed only in the revision and snapshot path. All 217 checkpoint arrays matched in dtype, shape and bytes, and the independent reader recovered the same balances. In the frozen run `lean-fr38-producer-replacement-20261122`, `owner-replay.log` retains the original failure. The repaired source is in `candidate-4099775c`, the native JSON and checkpoint in `native-fixed`, and the independent result in `producer-fixed.json`. [First replay](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/45e9668f3416cc8ac43e4e6c0f191ae162c43451/lean/PROGRESS.md#fr38-producer-replacement) · [Reader of the native result](checks/producer_replacement.py)

Cells Evidence located a human example of RNA persisting outside its source. Ng et al. detected placental lactogen mRNA in maternal plasma two hours after delivery in 4 of 13 women. The separate 24-hour group had 0 of 8 detectable. These denominators count women. The signal could include RNA released near delivery. An extracellular producer label and the fate of individual molecules remain open, and the Cells replacement fixture contains no extracellular RNA export. [Primary study, Figure 4](https://doi.org/10.1073/pnas.0637450100) · [Counts, timing and units](checks/data/ng2003_postpartum_rna.json)

<a id="fr38-marked-fates"></a>

## 22 November 2026 · Marked RNA at detachment and after label reuse

Lean proves that the durable and short-lived subsets of a finite set of marked detachments account for every event exactly once. In Cells’ continuous-amount model, a step sends fractions `p` and `1 − p` of gross release to the two fates. Lean proves that they sum to the marked gross amount over any half-open window, including when `p` changes between steps. Applying a separate survival fraction in [0, 1] to each fate leaves a combined surviving amount between zero and the original marked amount. [Lean proof](CellsProofs/Chemistry/MarkedRelease.lean) · [Mathlib finite partition](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/c44e0c8ee63ca166450922a373c7409c5d26b00b/Mathlib/Algebra/BigOperators/Group/Finset/Basic.lean#L117-L124)

On public engine `947ff262`, three compiled `resolved_pump_composite@1` worlds used `cells.templated_polymerisation@1` on the `resolved_pump_tissue@3` host. Two also used `cells.neutral_arrival_labels@1`. The hold started with 80 copies and there were zero new completions. Marking applied at release during [2, 4) and [6, 8) s. The durable fraction changed from 0.75 to 0.25 at 6 s. The two fates received 1.02212491320 × 10⁻¹⁰ fmol in the marked intervals, within 2.54 × 10⁻²³ fmol of the independently measured decrease in the hold. The unlabelled, reused-label and separate-label runs matched bit for bit across all 39 physical readouts at all 21 observations. Their diagnostics agreed. At 8 s the reused label held 5.10964032529 × 10⁻¹¹ fmol in the durable fate and 5.10570681890 × 10⁻¹¹ fmol in the short-lived fate. The maximum discrepancy from adding the two separately marked cohorts over all observations was 1.30 × 10⁻²⁶ fmol or less. [Comparison driver](checks/arrival_windows.py) · [Engine release and cohort definitions](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/947ff262c7fba53b53b32a7c44394e84be3fa897/docs/TEMPLATED_POLYMERISATION_LAWS.md#914-erratum-c-route-1-on-shares)

An independent counter-based reconstruction exposed a time-resolution limit. On a two-second output grid the short-lived cohort differed from the analytical integral by 4.80 × 10⁻⁸ relative at the last sample, above the 3 × 10⁻⁸ direct comparison tolerance. On the same run sampled every 0.5 s the maximum relative error fell to 2.94 × 10⁻⁹ or less. Both fates passed the direct comparison and the engine’s grid-error check. The run saves the errors on both grids. The synthetic point and interval controls also retained their earlier results, including a chase fraction of 6/8. [Grid estimate](../validation/gene_regulation/cohort.py#L179) · [Analytical reference](../validation/gene_regulation/release_check.py#L112)

Marking at completion was checked separately under both machine-return policies. Across 62 saved observations, the largest machine-pool error was 1.55 × 10⁻²⁰ fmol and the largest residue error was 1.19 × 10⁻¹⁹ fmol. The marked hold drained after its window closed, with material reaching both fates. In two lossless worlds the public reader recovered all four fate records, including the formerly missing short-lived RNA counter. The marked amount stayed equal to gross release inside [2, 8) s, reaching 7.66593665955 × 10⁻¹¹ fmol per fate. The ten-second total gain was 1.27765623623 × 10⁻¹⁰ fmol per fate. [Inventory and lossless replay](checks/terminal_release.py) · [Original failure](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/f11bfae57c7d3db1ac632b313cdb05dd863063de/lean/PROGRESS.md#fr38-lossless-fate) · [Public exporter repair](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/commit/36225a79)

Ntini et al. measured RNA in chromatin and nucleoplasmic fractions after a synthesis pulse. The authors’ ACTB row gives a 3.35 min chromatin-association half-time in human MCF-7 cells. Tutucci et al. counted ASH1 RNA spots per cell through mitosis in 21 yeast cells and reported 6.1 ± 2.8 min for the decline. The type of uncertainty is unspecified. These experiments give separate timescales for chromatin dissociation and RNA abundance. A direct test of the Cells chase fraction requires the amount marked at detachment and a later measurement of that same cohort. [Ntini et al., Figure 1](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cels.2023.09.005) · [ACTB result table](https://github.com/evgnt/chrTT-seq/blob/a86a76de0245fca5d52def6bd630904a4847497e/data/mRNA_allfeatures) · [Tutucci et al., Figure 6](https://doi.org/10.1038/nmeth.4502) · [Measurement definitions](checks/data/rna_chase_observables.json)

<a id="fr42-neutral-survival"></a>

## 22 November 2026 · Neutral labels in the compiled tissue model

For one step with constant arrivals and a common constant per-molecule loss rate, Lean proves that every positive final amount up to the starting amount plus gross arrivals determines exactly one nonnegative loss exponent. The upper endpoint gives zero loss. Each label ends with a nonnegative amount, all labels sum to the physical endpoint, and their normalized shares sum to one. A label with zero initial share and zero inflow stays empty. Without arrivals the shares of an occupied pool stay fixed. An empty pool supplies no information about a loss rate. The proof uses Mathlib’s exponential secant slopes, continuity at zero and intermediate value theorem. [Finite-step proof](CellsProofs/Chemistry/ArrivalSurvival.lean) · [Cells share update](../src/cells/numerics/label_shares.py)

On Cells `73afa19b`, the `resolved_pump_composite@1` model with the `resolved_pump_tissue@3` host ran for one second with and without `cells.neutral_arrival_labels@1`. The two runs matched bit for bit across all 42 shared history fields and 76 shared observations, including the energy records. The diagnostics also agreed. All five quadratic-loss pools kept their shares bit for bit. The three-unit pool ended at 0.7499996134802815 fmol, including 0.24999987116009384 fmol in the new cohort, against the exact 3/4 and 1/4 fmol. The analytic two-unit blank ends at 2/3 fmol, giving 1/12 fmol by subtraction. [Compiled comparison](checks/arrival_survival.py) · [Fixture and tolerances](../tests/unit/test_neutral_arrival_labels.py#L465)

The arrival window opened at 0.25 s and closed at 0.5 s. When the label was reused at 0.75 s, 0.08295689561 fmol of its earlier material remained in the cell. Old survivors plus new arrivals reached 0.16721168136 fmol at 1 s. Across seven observation times, the largest absolute difference from the independent constant-rate integrals was 2.78 × 10⁻¹⁷ fmol in the cell and 1.37 × 10⁻¹¹ fmol at the outside node. The largest relative discrepancy in the reporters’ physical balances was below 1.11 × 10⁻¹⁶. Separately, eight constructed steps covered zero starting stock, zero arrivals, zero loss, near-zero loss and rapid clearance on NumPy and CPU JAX. The maximum absolute share error was 1.11 × 10⁻¹⁶. The empty-row control preserved its stored shares. The frozen run `lean-fr42-neutral-survival-20261122` keeps the source in `record` and the results in `arrival-survival.json`. [Driver and analytical controls](checks/arrival_survival.py) · [Engine reference for the finite step](../src/cells/numerics/label_shares.py#L64)

Jovanovic et al. recorded separate medium and heavy Actn1 signals five hours after switching isotope medium in mock-treated mouse dendritic cells. The heavy fractions of the paired signals were 17.19% and 10.11% (mean 13.65%, sample SD 5.01 percentage points, two replicates). The denominator in each replicate is the sum of its two isotope channels. The study’s normalization cancels in that ratio. Recycled medium amino acids can enter newly synthesized protein. The measurements illustrate how to observe a labelled fraction. Testing the model’s common loss rate would require an independent measure of cohort turnover. [Primary study, Figure 1](https://doi.org/10.1126/science.1259038) · [GEO series GSE59792](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/geo/query/acc.cgi?acc=GSE59792) · [Four sample accessions and signal values](checks/data/jovanovic2015_actn1.json)

<a id="fr38-survival-readout"></a>

## 22 November 2026 · Comparing the two RNA survivor counts

For a cohort alive at the close of a release window, let `r` be the product of its subsequent survival factors. Lean proves that the absolute difference between the measured later count and `r × measured count at close` is at most `later error + r × close error`, given actual absolute bounds on both counts. A positive measured count at close makes this test equivalent to the ratio test. At zero the ratio is undefined. Cells estimates its numerical errors by comparing two observation grids. Whether those estimates bound the actual errors is a separate assumption. [Proof](CellsProofs/Chemistry/ArrivalWindow.lean) · [Reader and grid estimates](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/57c3cb909db5ebe9a24b90f3d78d7c6a182fd72b/validation/gene_regulation/cohort.py#L182-L218)

On `ce0ce9b2` the public checker accepted a synthetic short-lived RNA ratio of 1 against ¼ with only three observations. It assigned an infinite tolerance. A resolved empty cohort raised a division-by-zero exception. After the repair merged at `32c2f84d`, the same four controls reported insufficient resolution, a resolved mismatch, an empty cohort, and a passing calibrated turnover control. All eight saved R1 and R2 ratios passed on both revisions when reread from the original unlabelled engine exports at `da21173d`. Across both fates and both polymerase return policies, the largest absolute discrepancy in the ratio was 2.91 × 10⁻⁶. The largest discrepancy used 22.1% of its estimated allowance. The frozen run `lean-survival-rupture-mito-20261122` holds `rna-before.json` and `rna.json`. [Replay](checks/release_survival.py) · [Cells repair](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/commit/32c2f84d) · [Public checker](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/57c3cb909db5ebe9a24b90f3d78d7c6a182fd72b/validation/gene_regulation/release_check.py#L329-L357)

These eight development-export readouts precede the registered blind comparison. Its R1 failed and R2 was inconclusive. [Registered verdict and route-1 diagnosis](#fr38-extracellular-producer)

<a id="fr31-osmotic-rupture"></a>

## 22 November 2026 · Vesicle tension, area and time to first rupture

The registered D298 comparison passed five two-sided temperature checks and five one-sided osmotic checks. The osmotic rows constrain a lower bound on the rupture rate at the external tension. The neutral-DOPC fit also retains its reported 14–28% overprediction at the in-sample 7.5 mN/m setting. Interpreting the score assumes that first rupture records first pore opening and, in the osmotic comparison, comparable vesicle sizes across studies. [Registered verdict and scope](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/a6acbc27/docs/DECISIONS.md#d298-the-vesicle-rupture-blind-test-passes-set-1-five-of-five-set-2-five-of-five-one-sided-weak)

Cells Evidence found that Shibly et al.’s Figure 4A follows swelling and rupture **on the same neutral DOPC vesicles**. At a 3 mM osmotic difference and 4 mN/m of applied tension, the reported plateau radius increase was 0.86% (17 vesicles). The corresponding spherical area ratio is 1.01727. The paper reports 4.1 ± 0.1 mN/m of osmotic tension and 8.1 mN/m total. Its equation 6 with the printed mean modulus of 218 mN/m gives 3.7496 mN/m. The modulus itself has a 26 mN/m SD. Figure 4A displays selected relative-radius traces. The full cohort’s linked absolute areas and rupture or censoring times remain unavailable. Most Figure 4B vesicles ruptured before reaching a plateau. [Primary study, Figures 2 and 4](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.bpj.2016.09.043) · [Measured inputs](checks/data/shibly2016_swelling.json)

In Saha et al.’s charged 40:60 DOPG/DOPC vesicles at 25 °C, increasing the applied tension from 3.5 to 4.5 mN/m raised the mean rupture rate from 0.0046 to 0.030 s⁻¹, a factor of 6.52. The rupture assay used a 12 mOsm/L difference. A separate swelling cohort at 13 ± 5 mOsm/L yielded an independent geometry-based tension estimate of 3.0 ± 0.1 mN/m. The 2.6 ± 0.2 mN/m estimate in the rupture table comes from matching rates. These data support a cross-cohort comparison of held tension and rupture kinetics. Testing the area-distribution theorem still requires absolute initial sizes and survival from one cohort. The frozen calculation is `vesicle.json`. [Primary study, Tables 1 and 3](https://doi.org/10.1021/acs.jpcb.0c03681) · [Measurements](checks/data/saha2020_osmotic_rupture.json) · [Calculation](checks/vesicle_area.py)

<a id="mt1b-outer-admission"></a>

## 22 November 2026 · Combined charge with a conducting outer face

Two development worlds connected the seven-cycle matrix and finite intermembrane space (IMS) to a held bath through a potassium-only face. Their sole capacitor spanned matrix and IMS. Both completed two 1 ms steps. With matched potassium the initial IMS-minus-bath voltage was 0 mV. With 140 mM in the IMS and 5 mM in the bath it was −85.61293 mV, as required by potassium equilibrium at the fixture temperature. The maximum change in combined row charge was 1.27 × 10⁻¹⁸ fmol of charge equivalents. Omitting the IMS gave a change of 4.64 × 10⁻⁸ fmol in the same control. [Coupled development replay](checks/mitochondrial_outer_face.py) · [G5 initialization rule](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/57c3cb909db5ebe9a24b90f3d78d7c6a182fd72b/docs/MITOCHONDRIA_DESIGN.md#g5-the-outer-membranes-potential)

Lean also proves that summing charge over moving forms gives the base row’s charge plus the charge of the bound ligand receipts. Signed controls at the registered ADP, ATP and phosphate forms reproduce this identity. The public parser on `57c3cb90` admits K and AMP individually on this fixture and refuses H, Mg, ADP, ATP and Pi. The joint declaration stops at ADP because the face lacks a rule for its forms. That comparison used the original face. The later forms-aware law has its own [charge and carrier comparison](#forms-face-carriage). The full MT-1b anchor and start are separate tests. The frozen result is `mito.json`. <a id="fr38-release-energy"></a>

## 22 November 2026 · Heat booked at RNA detachment

FR-38 gives the held RNA and each physical fate the same standard energy per molecule at 1 M. Only the receiving row has a concentration-dependent energy. Writing `cap = volume × 1 M`, its concentration term is `RT n (ln(n/cap) − 1)`. Lean proves that its finite-step slope is nonpositive when both endpoint amounts are positive and at most `cap`, including a step with the same amount at both ends. Multiplying the opposite slope by each nonnegative detachment gives nonnegative heat for every split fraction. An inactive fate books zero release heat even while degradation changes its concentration. The conservation proof sums synthesis, detachment and degradation over any finite family of fate rows and counts the hold’s standard energy once. [Lean proof](CellsProofs/Chemistry/ReleaseEnergy.lean) · [Cells release law, §9.5–9.7](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/36b77006425c858c26727fa73181e7011d049a7c/docs/TEMPLATED_POLYMERISATION_LAWS.md#95-free-energy-and-heat)

Three compiled R1 controls released RNA from the initial hold with new synthesis and degradation off. At fractions 0, ½ and 1, the largest discrepancy against a 65-digit endpoint calculation was 4.36 × 10⁻²² pJ over 18 saved states. A fourth compiled world had synthesis and both degradation routes active. Its durable fraction switched from 0 to ½ to 1. The comparison reconstructed the intermediate operator state of each compiled step to price the two half-steps separately. Over 60 half-steps, the largest discrepancy for release heat was 1.30 × 10⁻²² pJ. For the entire state-function balance it was 3.83 × 10⁻¹⁹ pJ. There were 40 fate half-steps in which the amount fell and its release booked exactly zero. Booking detachment a second time would add 1.17 × 10⁻⁷ pJ over the run. In a separate component check, equal release and loss of 10⁻¹³ fmol left the fate amount unchanged and still booked 8.91 × 10⁻¹² pJ of release heat. [Replay](checks/release_energy.py) · [Engine route booking](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/36b77006425c858c26727fa73181e7011d049a7c/src/cells/operators/templated_polymerisation.py#L1355-L1400)

Bellecourt et al.’s wild-type RNA curve measures released terminator-length RNA divided by all terminator-length RNA. Its clock starts when elongation resumes upstream of the terminator. The plotted curve can constrain the combined arrival and release time course. Separate timing of completion is needed to isolate the dwell. It also supplies no measurement of release heat. [Primary study, Figure 6B](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6402830/#F6)

<a id="mt1b-combined-charge"></a>

## 22 November 2026 · The mitochondrial matrix and intermembrane-space charge

At fixed water volumes, the inventory is the sum of each species’ valence times its amount in the matrix and intermembrane space (IMS). Lean proves that its change equals the net signed charge imported across the outer boundary when internal chemistry conserves charge. It specializes the existing proof for all seven inner-membrane cycles to arbitrary signed extents. On a conducting passive face, zero total current determines one voltage. This voltage can be nonzero while individual ions move. Fixing the matrix charge alone leaves the initial total undetermined. The MT-1b declaration imposes zero outer voltage at the anchor and start states. Conservation carries the initialized total through the subsequent trajectory. [Lean proof](CellsProofs/Chemistry/MitochondrialCharge.lean) · [G4 and G5 anchor rule](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/36b77006425c858c26727fa73181e7011d049a7c/docs/MITOCHONDRIA_DESIGN.md#g5-the-outer-membranes-potential)

Two steps of the compiled seven-cycle world gave equal and opposite matrix and IMS charge changes within 6.31 × 10⁻²⁴ fmol of charge equivalents. A separate check exercised the generic compiled passive face with two mobile ions. Matched concentrations gave zero current. In the symmetric salt gradient the ions moved with total unsigned flux 0.002 fmol of charge equivalents per second and zero net charge flux. An unequal gradient required −8.19039 mV (IMS minus bath) for zero current to rounding. Forcing 0 mV in that control leaves an outward 0.001 fmol of charge equivalents per second. These are component results. The per-form outer-membrane closure and its coupling to the full MT-1b start state still need an integrated replay. [Comparison](checks/mitochondrial_charge.py) · [Component coverage](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/36b77006425c858c26727fa73181e7011d049a7c/validation/mitochondria/component_comparison.py#L35-L41)

Cells Evidence found a preparation-specific measurement by Abrantes et al. In intact cells from a human cell line, bulk IMS and cytosolic pH gave an inferred IMS-minus-cytosol potential of +33 ± 11 mV under proton equilibrium. This leaves the isolated-mitochondrion bath anchor unmeasured. [Primary study, Figure 1E and equation 1](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.mito.2026.102218) · [Measurements and scope](checks/data/abrantes2026_outer_voltage.json)

The frozen run `lean-release-energy-charge-20261122` records the compiled checks in `energy.json` and `charge.json` and their source revisions in `verification.json`.

<a id="fr38-terminal-inventories"></a>

## 22 November 2026 · Polymerase and RNA inventories at terminal release

In FR-38, a completed RNA enters a hold before it detaches into one of two fates. A chain with the `returned` policy frees its polymerase at completion. A chain with the `held` policy frees it at detachment. Lean proves conservation of the shared machine pool for any finite collection of chains with either policy. It also proves the residue balance through detachment. With initially empty stages and counters, residues supplied at stage exits plus residues already in the initial hold equal residues in unfinished chains, completed RNA still held, and all RNA detached so far. The two fates sum to total detachment at each update, even when their fractions change. [Lean proof](CellsProofs/Chemistry/TerminalRelease.lean) · [Engine ledgers](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/fc07c545836b155928aeca3e9ba74528d6e3b66d/src/cells/operators/templated_polymerisation.py#L1992-L2012)

The compiled R1S development worlds started with 80 held RNAs. They switched completion class at 20 s and durable fraction from 0.75 to 0.25 at 40 s. Across 31 saved times per policy, the largest absolute residuals were 1.55 × 10⁻²⁰ fmol of machines and 8.94 × 10⁻²⁰ fmol of residues. Independently reconstructed terminal-hold and fate-row balances also closed. Counting the initial hold under the opposite machine policy produced an 80-machine error with either policy. The frozen run `lean-release-inventories-20261122` records the inputs in `evidence` and the observed maxima in `inventories.json`. [Compiled replay](checks/terminal_release.py)

Harden et al. observed purified *E. coli* polymerase remaining on DNA more than four seconds after RNA departure in 87 of 100 selected traces. A separate set of recordings detected a second, antisense RNA in 78 of 114 traces with reverse polymerase motion and 5 of 257 without it. These observations distinguish RNA departure, DNA retention and productive reuse. They leave the return time to the Cells free pool unresolved. [Primary study, Figures 1 and 3](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6978322/) · [Counts and denominators](checks/data/harden2020_polymerase_retention.json)

<a id="mt1b-local-proton-read"></a>

## 22 November 2026 · Which compartment supplies the proton leak

Cortese, Voglino and Hackenbrock measured pH with soluble FITC-dextran in the intermembrane space (IMS) of intact isolated rat liver mitochondria. Relative to the external medium, IMS pH was 0.4–0.5 lower in the condensed configuration and 0–0.2 lower in the orthodox configuration. The reported bulk pH range was 6.0–8.5. These offsets imply IMS-to-medium proton activity ratios of 2.51–3.16 and 1.00–1.58 respectively. They are ranges across conditions. The abstract leaves the respiratory states and replicate uncertainties unresolved. The R2 reference reports IMS pH 0.009–0.019 **above** the bath at its uncoupled plateaus. Testing that sign and magnitude requires pH in both compartments under comparable uncoupling conditions. The paper supports distinguishing the compartments and supplies no leak-flux or reversal-voltage measurement. [Cortese et al. (1992), abstract](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/1610874/) · [Data and scope](checks/data/cortese1992_ims_ph.json) · [MT-1b declaration, erratum 2](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/5ef761505f2dbd48c35e36a2caee391e1f7d9394/docs/MITOCHONDRIA_DESIGN.md#L2232)

Four three-compartment FR-4 controls exercised the bounding patch in `resolved_pump_composite@1` with the `rapid_proton_buffer@1` read. The synthetic inside pH was 7.0. We independently set lumen pH to 6.65 or 7.115 and bath pH to 7.10 or 7.60. At both 0 and +15 mV, changing bath pH alone left inward flux identical. All eight clamps agreed with the 70-digit local-endpoint reference within 1.27 × 10⁻¹⁵ fmol/s. At 0 mV with lumen pH 6.65 and bath pH 7.10, Cells returned +5.8373 × 10⁻⁵ fmol/s. Substituting bath pH predicted −9.6921 × 10⁻⁶ fmol/s. In all four worlds the clamp at the step’s voltage reproduced its own flux exactly. Lean proves the change in flux at fixed voltage for any change in free outside concentration and strict increase for an open route. [Control and data conversion](checks/ims_proton_leak.py) · [Exact dependence](CellsProofs/Transport/ProtonLeak.lean)

At `5ef76150`, both the seven-cycle world and a control with general binding on the outside node alone parsed before adding the leak. Adding `proton_constant_field_leak@1` triggered the missing `rapid_binding@1` read at the matrix and at the isolated lumen endpoint respectively. The version-1 result is specific to that read. The [version-2 comparison](#mt1b-buffered-reversal) subsequently exercised buffered endpoints and measured reversal voltages. The evidence run `lean-ims-ph-replacement-20261122` records the eight readouts and both parser decisions in `ims-ph.json`. [Closure admission](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/5ef761505f2dbd48c35e36a2caee391e1f7d9394/src/cells/registry/species_registry.py#L1809)

Lean extends the FR-38 step balance to the event in the tagged §9.16 amendment that sends all unfinished residues and attached RNA out with a departing occupant and gives the newcomer empty stages and hold. ## 22 November 2026 · Pore rates, survival and heat under a tension hold

The replay runs `cells.assembly_lineages@2` in single-lineage and shared-pool population mode with `cells.pore_nucleation@1` and `cells.tension_hold@1` at engine revision `f51a3b07d302`. Lean derives the temperature derivative of the implemented log rate. For one fixed area and tension, the parser’s limit σ ≤ σβ/4 implies T ∂log k/∂T > 5/2. At 300 K the three scaled slopes were 33.7982, 21.5614 and 13.3245, the last at the upper tension. Independent one-sided differences stayed within 10⁻⁷ relative error. Doubling the area gave a rate ratio of 2.0000000000000036. For the two equally weighted founders, the one-second survival probabilities calculated from their rates averaged 0.357733. Survival at their mean area was 0.356163, and the endpoint mixture reproduced 0.357733. This compares probabilities implied by the engine rates, independently of the two sampled fates. [Lean proof](CellsProofs/Chemistry/VesiclePore.lean) · [Compiled runners and controls](checks/vesicle_pore.py)

The opening heat in units of k_BT equals log(k/P), with P the reverse collapse rate. The open-pore free energy cancels on release, including signed reservoir work. With tenfold reference viscosity, the single-vesicle rupture remained at 0.40421941134713335 s and the hold paid 12.23684452696 k_BT in both runs. Opening heat rose and release heat fell by 2.30258509299405 k_BT. The final recorded heat difference was zero. These fixtures have finite pools. The reservoir-work term is proved algebraically. [Event pair and declared viscosity](../docs/VESICLE_PORES_LAWS.md#7-the-event-pair-d287-opening-is-a-pair-no-second-exemption)

The published D298 verdict stands. Its five scored first-rupture rates from the Karal–Yamazaki temperature series passed two-sided. Shibly’s five osmotic rows passed a weaker one-sided test. The six fit rows had χ² = 98.6 on four degrees of freedom. These experiments use complete aspiration as the endpoint. Pore closure after an electrical pulse in free DOPC vesicles and a viscosity fit to thickness fluctuations at 25 °C constrain different quantities. Opening and release heat, and a temperature series of friction at a critical pore, remain unmeasured in these sources. [Registered verdict and premises](../docs/DECISIONS.md#d298-the-vesicle-rupture-blind-test-passes-set-1-five-of-five-set-2-five-of-five-one-sided-weak) · [Primary sources and measured quantities](checks/data/dopc_pore_observables.json)

### A rounded cutoff changes a founder’s fate

This section records the failure on `f51a3b07`. The same reproducer passed on public main after the clock repair. [Public retest](#fr31-pore-endpoint-repair)

The normal founder draw gives E = 0.3793660256513666 at k = 0.9385151108578917 s⁻¹. A hold beginning at 1 s and ending at 1.4042194113471334 s falls short of the exact crossing by 2.4228776836614065 × 10⁻¹⁷ s using those binary64 inputs. Both runners recorded the founder’s rupture at the cutoff, and both ledgers closed. At the immediately preceding binary64 cutoff both kept the founder alive. At the immediately following cutoff both recorded rupture, as the exact comparison predicts. A separate control with exactly sufficient exposure also ruptures at equality. The cutoff in the failing case was deliberately chosen next to the crossing. Its frequency in ordinary simulations is unknown. [Clock implementation](../src/cells/models/assembly_lineages_v2/pore_clock.py#L69) · [Exact inequality for the recorded inputs](CellsProofs/Chemistry/VesiclePore.lean)

The clock compares the rounded time `start + E/k` to the cutoff. Even `k * (end - start)` rounded to binary64 equals E in the failing case. The operator owner can preserve the inclusive rule by resolving ambiguous comparisons with the exact binary inputs before assigning the fate. Run `checks/vesicle_pore.py` in the Cells CPU environment to reproduce the JSON, including the exact rational exposure deficit at each cutoff. The script exits with status 1 when an observed fate differs from the exact reference. The frozen run `lean-pore-hold-20261122` keeps the source and machine-readable `pore.json`. <a id="fr31-area-bounds"></a>

## 22 November 2026 · FR-31 survival across vesicle sizes

At a common exposure `q` (per-area rupture rate integrated over the hold), a vesicle of fixed area `A` survives with probability `exp(−qA)`. For the initial population, let `m` be the weighted mean area. Lean proves the lower bound `exp(−qm)` by Mathlib’s finite Jensen inequality. At positive exposure the inequality is strict when two positive-weight areas differ. If each area lies between `L` and `U` with `L < U`, the upper bound is

`((U − m) exp(−qL) + (m − L) exp(−qU)) / (U − L)`.

A population at the two endpoints with those weights attains the upper bound. When the range has zero width, the single-area survival is exact. For spherical vesicles, the mean area is 4π times the mean of squared radii. Applying the bound to an experiment requires the initial cohort’s mean area and actual area limits. The bounds concern survival at a common exposure. Comparing fitted rupture rates or estimating an experimental discrepancy would require further data. [Tagged laws sheet, §5](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/c79e8ca4/docs/VESICLE_PORES_LAWS.md#5-the-area-and-the-clock) · [Lean proof](CellsProofs/Chemistry/VesicleArea.lean)

Four synthetic cases used a 60-digit exponential calculation against exact fractions. With equal counts at scaled areas 1 and 3 and exposure ln 2, survival is 5/16 against 1/4 from the mean area, and reaches the upper bound. Counts 1, 2, 1 at areas 1, 2, 3 give 9/32 between the same bounds. Equal areas and a zero-exposure control give equality. The experimental series and scoring rule remain those in D287. [Synthetic inputs and checks](checks/vesicle_area.py) · [Area and lifetime data search](claims/incoming/vesicle-osmosis-evidence.md#independent-fixed-tension-series)

## 22 November 2026 · Release windows, chase survival and reuse of a label

For a timestamped release, the left edge of a window counts and the right edge belongs to the next window. Each physical population assigns the same interval survival factor to its older material and to releases at the interval’s start. Lean proves that the contents of a reused label are the sum of the surviving histories. During a chase the original cohort is multiplied by the successive survival factors. Its denominator is the amount alive at window close, and a zero denominator gives an undefined fraction. [Lean proof](CellsProofs/Chemistry/ArrivalWindow.lean) · [Requester’s release and survival definitions](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/57cf9a4e/docs/FEATURE_REQUESTS.md#fr-38--rna-release-from-its-template-into-two-pools-with-different-lifetimes)

In the point-release example, 4 units finish synthesis in the first window and 10 detach in it. Of those 10, 8 survive to close and 6 to the chase sample, giving 6/8. At the end of the second use of the label, 3 old and 1 new unit remain. A separate host comparison has continuous production and uniform loss. Its interval totals give 5 surviving at close and 2.5 one interval later, with 6.25 across the reused label at the last sample. With the same total production, moving all arrivals to the interval’s start gives about 3.466 at close, while moving them arbitrarily close to the end approaches 6.931. The host calculation explicitly spreads production within each saved interval. [Reference calculations and compiled replay](checks/arrival_windows.py) · [Cells host counter reconstruction](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/57cf9a4e/validation/gene_regulation/cohort.py#L123)

The five-step compiled `resolved_pump_composite@1` fixture runs `cells.templated_polymerisation@1` in the `resolved_pump_tissue@3` host. At step starts 0, 2, 4, 6 and 8 s, the selected FR-38 release classes were 1, 2, 1, 2 and 1. Every newly detached molecule entered the selected class in both fates. The initial template hold supplied the releases and new chain completions stayed at zero. The host read the exported release counters. First-window survival from 4 to 6 s was 0.9998075 for the durable fate and 0.9992301 for the short-lived fate. In both, the two reconstructed surviving amounts summed to one integration across the gap within 2 × 10⁻¹⁴ relative tolerance. These are development class rows. The later FR-42 tissue comparison checked physical and energy neutrality on the share-based engine. Following an FR-38 producer across replacement and restart remains open. [Compiled neutral labels](#fr42-neutral-survival) [Compiled operator](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/57cf9a4e/src/cells/operators/templated_polymerisation.py#L1209) · [FR-42 design and acceptance](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/57cf9a4e/docs/NEUTRAL_LABELS_DESIGN.md#6-the-requesters-acceptance-checks-and-how-each-would-be-built)

Bellecourt et al. counted released terminator-length RNA separately from RNA still associated with the template. Their reported wild-type release plateau in reducing conditions was 94 ± 2%. The denominator was all terminator-length RNA at sampling. This supplies an observable for detachment. It gives neither paired single-molecule completion and release times nor the fraction of the released cohort alive later. [Primary study, Figure 5A](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6402830/#F5) · [Measurement and denominator](checks/data/bellecourt2019_rna_release.json)

The corrected range comparison exercises `cells.assembly_lineages@1` with the version 2 pair-consistent catalysis law named explicitly. On the engine containing `228a5ef9`, all nine cases passed. The parser refused 2¹⁰¹⁷ at the strict upper boundary and also refused the former 2¹⁰²² control and the original 2¹⁰²³ overflow witness. The compiled cache preserved the smallest normal tick, 2⁻¹⁰²². <a id="fr42-cohort-shares"></a>

## 22 November 2026 · FR-42 arrival-time cohorts under a common loss rate

For a well-mixed physical population of size `N`, a cohort of size `n` receives the share `J(N)·n/N` of the population’s loss. The empty population has zero loss. Lean proves that the shares sum to the physical loss and that one shared loss step preserves the pooled total for any number of labels, including an unlabelled remainder. Any readout evaluated solely from the physical totals before and after the step therefore also agrees. Energy and heat must use those pooled inputs for this result to apply. [Lean proof](CellsProofs/Chemistry/ArrivalCohort.lean) · [FR-42 design target](../docs/FEATURE_REQUESTS.md#fr-42--neutral-arrival-time-labels-that-leave-the-dynamics-unchanged)

For quadratic loss, Lean verifies the derivative and initial value of the survivor curve `b / (1 + a·t)` for a cohort starting at `b` in a pool starting at `a`. With two old units and one new unit, the total at time one is 3/4 and the new cohort holds 1/4. The separate two-unit blank ends at 2/3, giving a difference of 1/12. Computing the initial loss separately for the two labels would give 5 against the physical value of 9. This was the design reference for the subsequent compiled FR-42 comparison, including window boundaries, label reuse and integration tolerances. [Compiled replay](#fr42-neutral-survival) [FR-38 erratum B](../docs/TEMPLATED_POLYMERISATION_LAWS.md#913-erratum-b-the-label-rows-are-not-neutral-labels)

<a id="vesicle-birth-returns"></a>

## 22 November 2026 · Reconstructing rupture at birth from the shear records

A single-lineage shear records the pool before its followed daughter empties. A population shear records the pool after all daughters that ruptured at that stop have emptied. Lean reuses the R9 release proof to sum the receipts of the selected daughters at each destination. The finite receipt is bounded by the final count, so subtracting it recovers the earlier pool. Adding the held receipt to the final signed reservoir supply recovers the earlier supply. A daughter sent to the single-lineage sink contributes no receipt. The proof assumes each daughter and source place is listed once and the release flags identify the retained daughters that ruptured. [Lean proof](CellsProofs/Chemistry/VesicleBirth.lean) · [R10 record definition](../validation/assembly_lineages_v2/FORMAT_OL2.md#L570)

Six compiled `cells.assembly_lineages@2` runs on `b99d6315` closed their energy accounts. The reader matched daughter keys, counts, row times, heat and reservoir work in the saved bundles. [Comparison driver](checks/vesicle_birth.py)

| Case | Saved result |
| --- | --- |
| The followed daughter ruptures, with a finite or held tracer pool | The shear row has 20 pool lipids. The end row has 26. The five returned tracers raise the finite pool from 0 to 5 or reduce held supply by 5. |
| Only the unfollowed daughter ruptures | The retained daughter stays in slot 0. The sister leaves with six lipids and six tracers. The finite lipid pool stays at 20 and the finite tracer pool at 0. |
| Two parents split at the same stop, with a finite or held tracer pool | Two distinct daughters occupy slot 2 in succession. Their receipts contain six and five tracers. The final lipid pool of 32 recovers the starting 20 after subtracting both receipts. |
| Two daughters of one three-way split rupture | Each returns six lipids. The two held tracer receipts of five and six reduce supply by 11. The split attributes the two release work terms and the lysis rows book them once. |

The same reader checked both shears in the archived `ol3_shear_probe_run` on `e6571f88`. At 0.01 s no daughter ruptured. At 0.02 s, seven ruptures raised the finite lipid pool from 270 to 2,359 and the solute pool from 0 to 55,631. The held lipid pool kept its count and its signed supply fell by 356. The first individual return contained 293 finite lipids and 7,975 solute molecules. The reader reconciled every intervening split and release row. [Registered probe runner](../validation/assembly_lineages_v2/probes2.py) · [Engine revision](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/commit/e6571f88ead17ef235bd4fb2c1f26f387f7c1967)

Six planted errors were rejected, including swapped keys in the reused slot, a credit for the unfollowed sister, a reversed held-supply sign, doubled release work, and reading either runner’s shear pool at the wrong point. Reordering the lookup entries with the keys intact preserved the result.

**Biological scope.** Deshpande et al. imaged a DOPC-based liposome splitting at a Y-junction. One daughter remained intact while the other ruptured. Figure 3b pairs the two retained daughters’ tracer signals in successful divisions. Figure 4 reports external dye spreading averaged across events. A calibrated measurement of the parent, both daughters and the surrounding liquid in the same asymmetric rupture remains open. [Sibling fates, Fig. 1d](https://ceesdekkerlab.nl/wp-content/uploads/2019/08/Mechanical-division-of-cell-sized-liposomes.pdf#page=2) · [Paired daughter signals, Fig. 3b](https://ceesdekkerlab.nl/wp-content/uploads/2019/08/Mechanical-division-of-cell-sized-liposomes.pdf#page=5) · [External fluorescence, Fig. 4](https://ceesdekkerlab.nl/wp-content/uploads/2019/08/Mechanical-division-of-cell-sized-liposomes.pdf#page=6)

<a id="vesicle-lysis-returns"></a>

## 22 November 2026 · Molecules returned by a terminal rupture

The assembly record gives two states at a rupturing step. The diagnostic event gives the pool after the triggering step. The end row for a single lineage, or the matching lysis row in a population, gives the pool after the vesicle empties. Lean proves that applying the individual returns to the first state increases each finite pool by its total receipt. A held pool keeps its count and its signed reservoir supply decreases by that receipt. Summing receipts over the destinations recovers the entire cargo. [Lean proof](CellsProofs/Chemistry/VesicleRelease.lean) · [R9 record definition](../validation/assembly_lineages_v2/FORMAT_OL2.md#L545)

On engine `87e81f56`, all four compiled `cells.assembly_lineages@2` runs closed their energy accounts and their serialized records reconciled by place name. In both modes, one lipid had already detached into the pool. Four membrane lipids and two lumen lipids returned to that same place, giving **1 + 6 = 7**. Counting the receipt a second time would give 13. The two impermeant tracer molecules increased a finite pool by two. With a held destination they reduced signed supply by two and booked **9.210340371976182 kT** of reservoir work. The other population slot survived. These controls cover event-triggered lysis. [Run and check the four bundles](checks/vesicle_release.py) · [Lineage release](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/87e81f56366b5d06bd2abd45ebd2bb45a70f8b25/src/cells/models/assembly_lineages_v2/runner.py#L492) · [Population release](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/87e81f56366b5d06bd2abd45ebd2bb45a70f8b25/src/cells/models/assembly_lineages_v2/population.py#L700)

| Preparation and measured quantity | Experimental result | Cells comparison |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Fujiwara and Yanagisawa, five DOPE/DOPC liposomes. Final volume relative to starting volume, 70 to 285 mOsm/L. | About 0.25, from the reported fourfold inverse-volume plateau. Paired measurements on an individual liposome support GFP retention. | **0.2456** with the prepared osmolarities and fixed osmoles, without fitting. Compatible at the reported precision. [Study](https://doi.org/10.1021/sb4001917) · [Tracer and volume, Fig. S3](https://doi.org/10.1021/sb4001917.s001) |
| Wolfisberg et al., one weakly adhered POPC vesicle. Volume relative to the spherical capacity at the measured area. | **0.30** at 58 mOsm/L. All four later measurements lay below the respective predictions. | **0.4393** at that point after calibrating to the first observation. [Figure 2B and inputs](checks/data/wolfisberg2025_vesicle.json) |
| Bhatia et al., one trapped vesicle with an inward tube. Final lumen relative to the measured start, 66 to 72 mM. | At most **0.756** using the central outer contour. Subtracting a straight cylindrical tube gives an estimated **0.705**. | **0.917** assuming retained osmoles. The reported ±1,000 µm³ outer-volume uncertainty spans the roughly 950 µm³ difference. The new compiled endpoint replay gave an initial-to-final ratio of **4.071428571428582**. The five-volume plateau and the paired GFP plot have different observations. The reported linear fit in the latter has R² = 0.9987. The error of an individual retention estimate and the retention of smaller osmoles remain open. <a id="ol5-event-energy"></a>

## 22 November 2026 · Osmotic energy booked by a single vesicle event

The fixed-area law uses the smaller of the equilibrium osmotic volume and the spherical volume allowed by the membrane. Lean proves that the incremental energy equals the final free energy minus the initial free energy for any two positive counts and footprints. The proof covers both sides of the volume limit, both directions across it and an exactly full vesicle. At a branch switch the correction is proportional to `x − 1 − log x`, where `x` is spherical volume divided by free osmotic volume at the start. This is nonnegative and vanishes at the boundary. Pricing an event and then reversing it sums to zero in exact arithmetic. The empty lumen and the infinite energy of solute without membrane have separate engine checks. [Event proof](CellsProofs/Chemistry/VesicleEvent.lean) · [Underlying energy](CellsProofs/Chemistry/Vesicle.lean) · [Incremental engine formula](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/329e9e395ac117998657a21eb827192d34314107/src/cells/models/assembly_lineages_v2/lumen.py#L193-L242)

On `cells.assembly_lineages@2`, the comparison prices 169 distinct directed state pairs. These include 13 crossings in each direction, the saved transitions of a 96-event public run and 18 four-event cycles. The reference uses 70-digit physical volumes and subtracts the absolute energies before rounding the result. The old compiled calculation had a maximum error of **1.70 × 10⁻¹⁰ kT**. On OL5 candidate `329e9e39` it was **1.07 × 10⁻¹⁴ kT**. The maximum reverse-pair residual and the maximum closed-cycle residual were both **5.33 × 10⁻¹⁵ kT**. The synthetic controls exactly on the branch boundary agreed to the displayed float64 precision. [CPU comparison, energy case](checks/vesicle_events.py) · [Engine fixture](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/329e9e395ac117998657a21eb827192d34314107/tests/unit/test_assembly_lineages_v2_lumen.py#L141-L231)

<a id="ol5-short-draws"></a>

### Ordered draws in a reaction rate

The engine sums at most three logarithms to price draws without replacement. Lean connects the masked sum to Mathlib’s descending factorial for every feasible count, including a zero-arity side and an exactly exhausted side. On 46 feasible inputs through 2²⁰ molecules, both the mass-action and phase-exchange directions were within **3.55 × 10⁻¹⁵** of the logarithm of the exact integer product. Both backends masked all six impossible draws. The previous table-difference calculation had a maximum absolute log error of **1.20 × 10⁻⁹** on the same synthetic inputs. These cases isolate the rate factors. The registered OL5 verdict has its own runner and tolerances. [Rate proof](CellsProofs/Chemistry/AssemblyKinetics.lean) · [Engine log products](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/329e9e395ac117998657a21eb827192d34314107/src/cells/models/assembly_lineages_v2/mass_action.py#L76-L90) · [OL5 decision](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/329e9e395ac117998657a21eb827192d34314107/docs/DECISIONS.md#L8945)

<a id="bhatia-vesicle-deflation"></a>

### What the outer contour measures after osmotic deflation

Bhatia et al. imaged one giant vesicle before and after transfer from 66 to 72 mM. Figure 2 gives about 1,300 µm² and 4,500 µm³ initially. After deflation the outer contour enclosed 3,400.9 ± 1,000 µm³, including bath-origin liquid in an inward tube. The central measurements put the final lumen at **at most 0.756 of the starting volume**, independent of tube shape. A straight cylinder 18 µm long and 4 µm in diameter gives about 226 µm² of membrane and displaces about 226 µm³. Including it gives an estimated final area of 1,330 µm² and a lumen fraction of **0.705**. The tube dimensions have no independent uncertainty estimate. [Figure 2, p. 7363](https://doi.org/10.1039/D0SM00155D) · [Movie S1 identifies the bath](https://www.rsc.org/suppdata/d0/sm/d0sm00155d/d0sm00155d1.pdf#page=2)

Assuming initial osmotic balance, conserved internal solute and fixed membrane area, the compiled comparison predicts **4,125 µm³**, or **0.917** of the measured starting volume. The cylinder estimate is about 950 µm³ lower, within the quoted ±1,000 µm³ on the outer volume alone. Even the starting central measurements give a reduced volume of 1.021 against a spherical maximum of 1. The authors estimate about 25% uncertainty in reconstructed area and 33% in volume. This specimen leaves the quantitative model comparison unresolved. Figure 4 used a separate 92 to 117 mM exchange. Its internal concentration was inferred from volume under a conserved-solute assumption. [Measurements and assumptions](checks/data/bhatia2020_vesicle_deflation.json) · [Figures 2 and 4](https://www.mpikg.mpg.de/th/people/dimova/publications/Bhatia%2020%20SoftMater.pdf#page=5)

<a id="gard-v2-cache"></a>

## 22 November 2026 · GARD v2 preserves the smaller catalyst

For `cells.gard_catalysis_pair_consistent@2`, measure each row sum in units of its lowest set bit. Lean proves that the parser’s 106-bit precision bound puts every legal composition on a grid where the two compensation terms add exactly. The proof covers a signed update, successive updates, founder/daughter folds and recovery of the reverse numerator. It uses the published error-free TwoSum contract and nearest rounding. A finite exponent range and preservation of low words are additional device requirements. The proof allows either choice at a tie and constructs a model satisfying its arithmetic assumptions. [Lean proof](CellsProofs/Chemistry/GardCache.lean) · [Engine arithmetic](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/6b28ec55368011de79dce85e6172b5844b77a287/src/cells/models/assembly_lineages/gard.py#L291-L357) · [TwoSum, Theorem 3.4](https://doi.org/10.1137/030601818)

On the original stress case (diagonal 2⁵⁴, other catalyst 1), the compiled host returned join and reverse factors **2 and 2** and exactly **0 kT** of heat. Version 1’s recorded false refusal remains in its own claim. Across 22 signed updates, each cache matched a fresh fold byte for byte and its two words summed to the rational reference. All adjacent factor pairs matched bit for bit, including the empty-parent boundary. A 106-bit row was admitted and a 107-bit row refused. The 2²⁰ count case exercised the compiled cache directly. At the larger counts a multiplier differed by one float64 spacing from a scalar evaluation that rounds the division separately. [Compiled comparison](checks/gard_cache.py) · [Version 1 record](claims/gard-pair-heat.md)

<a id="gard-v2-range"></a>

### Two exponent limits and an explicit-capacity refusal

| Input | Expected | Compiled Cells result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Two molecules with coefficient 2¹⁰²², capacity 8 | Finite cache and positive event rate | One event, with a finite cache |
| Same composition with coefficient 2¹⁰²³ | A valid event with finite rates or a domain refusal | Parser admitted a four-bit row. Both cache words became NaN. The run returned `stalled`, zero events and a passing empty heat ledger |
| Single coefficient 2⁻¹⁰⁷⁴ and one molecule | Preserve one tick or refuse the value | NumPy preserved it and the compiled CPU cache returned zero. Both factors round to one at this scale |
| Two types and an explicit molecule capacity equal to fission size | 4,096 and 4,097 are both within the declared 2²⁰ limit | 4,096 parsed. At 4,097 the default type-bucket lookup raised a configuration error despite the explicit capacity |

The independent rational propensity sum in the overflow case rounds to **25,165,824** in the model’s nominal inverse-time units. The half-sized control gives 12,582,912. The row-bit guard measures the width of the significand. This example also needs a bound on its physical exponent. The capacity finding arises earlier, when Python evaluates the default argument to the execution dictionary lookup. Run `checks/gard_cache.py --range` for all three findings. A nonzero exit preserves them in the numerical-failures table. [Precision guard](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/6b28ec55368011de79dce85e6172b5844b77a287/src/cells/models/assembly_lineages/gard.py#L359-L396) · [Execution parser](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/6b28ec55368011de79dce85e6172b5844b77a287/src/cells/models/assembly_lineages/spec.py#L358-L371) · [Reproducer](checks/gard_cache.py)

<a id="polymerisation-balances"></a>

## 22 November 2026 · Free machines, unfinished chains and released products

One machine moves into the first stage at initiation and returns to its pool on the last exit. For any finite set of chains sharing a pool, Lean proves that internal stage transfers cancel. It also proves that the residues added at stage exits equal those still in unfinished chains plus those in released products, relative to the starting ledger. Stage j, counted from zero, carries j times the residues per stage. Both identities hold for arbitrary stage extents, including the SSPRK half-step used by the operator. [Proofs](CellsProofs/Chemistry/Polymerisation.lean) · [Engine update](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/6b28ec55368011de79dce85e6172b5844b77a287/src/cells/operators/templated_polymerisation.py#L862-L889) · [Native balances](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/6b28ec55368011de79dce85e6172b5844b77a287/src/cells/operators/templated_polymerisation.py#L1378-L1387)

Three 30 s CPU worlds on `resolved_pump_composite@1` exercised the two-route A1 model, competing chains and a one-stage chain beside an eight-stage chain. Across 183 saved rows the largest machine residual was **5.80 × 10⁻¹⁹ fmol** and the largest residue residual **3.39 × 10⁻²⁰ fmol of residues**. In the two-gene control, leaving one chain out of the shared machine pool produced a 4.88 × 10⁻⁸ fmol error. The one-stage chain retained zero residues in transit and released 6.55 × 10⁻⁵ fmol of residues. Source and run records for both new comparisons are in `lean-gard-poly-balances-20261122`. [Compiled comparisons](checks/polymerisation_balances.py)

Li et al. measured 31 ± 3%, 51 ± 3% and 65 ± 4% of ribosomes bound to mRNA at the same measured growth rate under carbon, nitrogen and phosphorus limitation respectively (mean ± SEM, three biological replicates). Bound and unbound shares have a common denominator. These data constrain how engagement changes with the preparation. Stanners separately measured label in nascent and completed proteins in hamster cells. The signals crossed at 34, 32, 40 and 32.5 s. A quantitative test of that time course would need the experimental tracer normalization. Neither dataset was used to fit these synthetic worlds. [Li et al., Fig. 1e and Table 1](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41564-018-0199-2) · [Stanners, Fig. 4 and Table II](https://doi.org/10.1016/S0006-3495(68)86487-2) · [Measurement records](checks/data/polymerisation_pool_measurements.json)

<a id="fr33-label-energy"></a>

## 21 November 2026 · FR-33, heat booked when neutral labels mix

Two neutral labels can cross a face in opposite directions while the total tracer stays still. For any finite collection of strictly positive label fractions with common diffusivity, Lean proves that resolving labels adds a nonnegative term to the ideal face power. The term is zero exactly when every label has the same fraction on both sides. The reduction from the Cells neutral face uses its logarithmic quotient or equal-concentration branch for every active row and the parent. An optional check identifies the term with Mathlib’s relative-entropy function. [Proof](CellsProofs/Transport/LabelMixingPower.lean) · [Mathlib equivalence](checks/mixing/KLFun.lean)

The accepted step books heat from applied fluxes and predicted weights. With positive weights this booking is zero exactly when every applied flux is zero. Combining two rows with their summed weights removes a nonnegative square. An independently simulated parent predicts its own weight. We therefore ran both representations through the public two-node world. The table gives cumulative face heat after 1 s at a 10 ms step. Label amounts summed to the parent at both nodes throughout, within 3 × 10⁻¹⁴ fmol. [Accepted-step booking](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/ee0b8106406eb26e57ab1b0c34e16ed6c3f83a2b/src/cells/models/surface_pump_v3.py#L2119-L2134) · [Comparison](checks/label_mixing_power.py)

| Gradients | Parent (pJ) | Separate labels (pJ) |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Opposing 90:10 fractions, equal totals | 0 | 1.47315444 |
| Same fractions, twofold total gradient | 0.33000851 | 0.33000851 |
| Different fractions and different totals | 0.11645669 | 0.51387347 |

Across 800 accepted CPU float64 steps the largest difference between a recorded heat increment and its independent reconstruction was 3.68 × 10⁻¹⁶ pJ. In the opposing case a 5 ms step gave 1.47318357 pJ against the analytic continuous free-energy loss of 1.47319327 pJ. D1 users who split one chemical species into separate rows for tracking should keep this change in calculated heat explicit. Its interpretation as measurable heat from a label alone remains open. The frozen numerical record is `lean-label-energy-wall-20261121/label-energy.json`. [Parent and label transport](#fr33-label-diffusion)

<a id="fr28-wall-wash"></a>

## 21 November 2026 · FR-28, bound tracer at a wash and at the next reading

A draw removes a fraction of the free tracer. Applying that fraction to the whole inventory overestimates the sample by the same fraction of the bound stock. Lean proves retention at the event, inventory closure during later exchange, and the sign of the first binding rate after dilution at an initially equilibrated surface. [Proof](CellsProofs/Tissue/WallWash.lean) · [Compiled comparison](checks/wall_wash.py)

The operator candidate `9917e3d9` started with 60 fmol free, 10 on the wall and 30 on the specimen. A half wash collected **30 fmol** and the refill supplied 0.0000005 fmol. All eight site-state fields were byte-identical across the event. The post-wash wall rate was −2.99999995 fmol/s. At the next recorded step, 10 ms later, the wall held **9.97015402 fmol** and the specimen held 29.99100988 fmol. Their releases raised the liquid to 30.03883660 fmol. Node ownership and an inert stand-in yielded identical traces. A concentration-matched refill gave zero wall rate. A whole draw collected 60 fmol and left 40 fmol bound at the instant of exchange. The frozen numerical record is `lean-label-energy-wall-20261121/wall-wash.json`. [Wash and counters](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/9917e3d9567993e1019c8caafa89f8653ee6ea9b/src/cells/operators/wash_schedule.py#L438-L501) · [Candidate well](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/9917e3d9567993e1019c8caafa89f8653ee6ea9b/validation/tissue/wash_schedule_fixtures.py#L56-L81)

Robinson et al. separately counted labelled albumin in removed urine, six water rinses and the final disk. A separate five-hour experiment on the same plastic at pH 5 and 87 mg/L yielded **8.759 µg** in pooled liquid and **22.96 ng/cm²** on the disk (0.22%, four replicates). That pool included the original sample, rinses and pipette tip. These measurements support separate liquid and surface accounting. Testing the simulated release rate requires known rinse times and a way to distinguish desorption from liquid carried over between rinses. [Rinse series, Fig. S3](https://stacks.cdc.gov/view/cdc/50419/cdc_50419_DS2.pdf#page=4) · [Surface data, Table S2](https://stacks.cdc.gov/view/cdc/50419/cdc_50419_DS2.pdf#page=7) · [Liquid data, Table S4](https://stacks.cdc.gov/view/cdc/50419/cdc_50419_DS2.pdf#page=9) · [Measurement record](checks/data/robinson2014_wall_wash.json)
