<a id="fr33-label-diffusion"></a>

## 21 November 2026 · FR-33 D1, diffusion of a label and its complement

For a neutral tracer with a common Fick coefficient, summing the fluxes of any finite set of labels recovers the parent flux. Opposing gradients can move labels even when the parent is stationary. Lean proves this for ideal Fick transport, neutral constant-field permeation and the Cells neutral face when every row and the parent use the logarithmic quotient or have equal end concentrations. The small nonzero-gradient polynomial branch has a numerical check. [Proof](CellsProofs/Transport/LabelDiffusion.lean) · [Compiled comparison](checks/label_diffusion.py)

In the two-node world at `1295a7d7`, total tracer on the right stayed at 1 fmol. Right-hand labelled tracer rose from 0.1 to 0.299998884 fmol at 1 s, against 0.3 analytically. The sum of both labels equalled the parent at both nodes on all 101 rows. On a separate face with valence +1, +10 mV from left to right and opposing 90:10 label fractions, the row sum was 2.72590648 × 10⁻⁵ fmol/s versus 3.74339294 × 10⁻⁵ fmol/s for the parent. Lean gives their ratio exactly as (8/5)/ln(9), about **0.7281913813**. Independent charged faces therefore change the total flux in this case. Gray et al. observed labelled PSD-95 leaving a spine and appearing in two neighboring spines. The measurement supports redistribution and leaves the equal-mobility assumption untested. [Engine face](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/1295a7d794feb508bdf565ca2244aff3380b27d6/src/cells/numerics/surface_transport.py#L280-L301) · [Gray et al., Fig. 4A](https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pbio.0040370.g004)

<a id="fr37-regional-sampling"></a>

## 21 November 2026 · FR-37, when a dye draw represents the chamber

For two internally well-mixed regions and a nonempty draw, Lean proves that sample and chamber concentrations agree exactly when the two concentrations agree or the same fraction of each region is withdrawn. A larger draw fraction from the richer region enriches the sample. Each region’s final amount plus its collected amount equals its initial amount plus its refill. The local draw volumes are inputs. [Proof](CellsProofs/Tissue/RegionalSampling.lean) · [Compiled comparison](checks/regional_sampling.py) · [Cells example at the tested revision](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/ee0b8106406eb26e57ab1b0c34e16ed6c3f83a2b/validation/sampling/uneven_draw.py)

| Starting excess A / B (fmol) | Draw A / B (pL) | Collected excess (fmol) | Excess left A / B (fmol) | Sample excess (mM) |
| --- | --- | ---: | --- | ---: |
| 8 / 2 | 100 / 0 | 8 | 0 / 2 | 0.080 |
| 5 / 5, initially mixed | 50 / 50 | 5 | 2.5 / 2.5 | 0.050 |
| 8 / 2 | 75 / 25 | 6.5 | 2 / 1.5 | 0.065 |

Each region held 100 pL. All three compiled runs closed the gross local inventory at every recorded time. The refill supplied 10⁻¹⁰ fmol of background dye per draw. The rounded zero in the first row was 8.27 × 10⁻¹⁸ fmol above background. Cells took the regional draw volumes as declared. Johnson et al. measured enrichment independently in a physical flow experiment. Equal outlet flow gave approximately 75% and 25% of stock dye concentration in the two branches, with 50% expected under complete mixing. The experiment measured two flowing outlets. An independent measurement of the dye remaining in each region after a finite draw is still open. [Johnson et al., Fig. 5a and paragraph 33](https://doi.org/10.1029/2005JB004087) · [Measurement record](checks/data/johnson2006_branch_dye.json)

Run the two comparison scripts with the Cells CPU environment and the tested checkout’s `src` and root on `PYTHONPATH`. <a id="fr33-regional-tracer"></a>

## 21 November 2026 · Where the original protein label goes after a pulse

The FR-33 tracer splits a node’s outflow according to the cohort’s share across the start and end of each accepted step. Lean starts from the tracer’s linear equations. It proves that the cohort’s change in any fixed region plus its local clearance equals labelled transfer across its boundary. For a closed cell after the pulse ends, **label inside + label in the rest of the cell + clearance since the pulse = label present at the pulse endpoint**. New synthesis can continue under a different label. Each route must record one-way movement. A two-way net flux alone can hide exchange of labels in opposite directions. [Lean proof](CellsProofs/Tissue/RegionalTracer.lean#L47-L108) · [Solver and bookings](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/d48d40dd1ce03c1093a1c27e775cd2e8239ccf34/validation/regions/readouts.py#L534-L548)

On engine `d48d40dd`, the compiled `resolved_pump_composite@1` example on `resolved_pump_tissue@3` exercised compartment ownership, one-way exchange and first-order turnover. Across 501 rows the donor cohort’s largest whole-cell balance residual was **5.20 × 10⁻¹⁸ fmol** for 0.001 fmol initially labelled. The tracer’s donor transfer matched the sampled engine counter at every output. For the cohort made during the pulse, 5.327 × 10⁻⁵ fmol had already cleared by the 0.25 s switch. Using only subsequent clearance closed its balance to **2.71 × 10⁻¹⁹ fmol** across the remaining 251 rows, while the cell continued to synthesize protein. These are accounting residuals against the computed trace. The route split at a mixing node is a time discretization. [Comparison](checks/regional_tracer.py) · [Pulse and chase fixture](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/d48d40dd1ce03c1093a1c27e775cd2e8239ccf34/validation/regions/example.py#L49-L73)

<a id="fr33-matched-donors"></a>

### What distinguishes transfer from degradation

Lean proves that one-way transfer and pure clearance give the same entire donor trace for the same starting amount and rate. For positive time, rate and starting amount, the receiver gain in the transfer arm equals the clearance in the degradation arm. In the compiled control the donors agreed exactly at all 11 outputs. Both started at 0.001 fmol and held 0.000606531 fmol at 0.5 s. [Two-control proof](CellsProofs/Tissue/RegionalTracer.lean#L119-L146) · [Maintained Cells control](../validation/compartment_transfer/README.md)

| Control at 0.5 s | Receiver gain (fmol) | Cumulative loss (fmol) |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Internal transfer | 0.000393469 | 0 |
| Local degradation | 0 | 0.000393469 |

Gray et al. photoactivated PSD-95 in one dendritic spine and observed fluorescence fall there and rise in two neighboring spines. Their green/red ratios measure redistribution in those spines. Uchigashima et al. mapped pre-existing and nascent PSD-95 at 3,239–4,402 synaptic regions per neuron in three neurons after a two-hour interval. Neither observation measures the old label in the source, its full complement and the whole neuron across a 48-hour chase. The protein in the Cells study request is unnamed. PSD-95 provides a comparison for the mechanism, and its imaging data supply no fitted Cells rate. [Gray et al., Fig. 4A](https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pbio.0040370.g004) · [Uchigashima et al., Fig. 6](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12606364/#Fig6) · [Measurement records](checks/data/regional_tracer_measurements.json)

<a id="matched-buffer-heat"></a>

## 21 November 2026 · Finite-pool heat when the buffer matches the starting concentration

Let a pool place hold `n` molecules, and let the buffer supply exactly `b = nf` molecules in the withdrawn volume fraction `0 < f < 1`. For every realized withdrawal, the heat booked by FR-35 lies between zero and that outcome’s physical split score. Lean proves this by comparing adjacent binomial masses. When the word sampler’s withdrawal probability equals `f`, averaging gives **0 < E[H] ≤ entropy of the withdrawn count ≤ ln(n+1)** for a nonempty place. Along any growing sequence of exactly matched pools, E[H]/n tends to zero. The sign and the per-outcome comparison also hold after summing over matched places. The mean bound uses independent uniform words and natural logarithms. An independent experimental energy budget for this exchange remains open. [Lean proof](CellsProofs/Chemistry/MatchedBuffer.lean) · [Discrete energy convention and sampler](#fr35-withdrawal-energy) · [Cells design](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/2ae9ce8e5c6b56ecab091b6167d034eb97a5520f/docs/ASSEMBLY_WITHDRAWAL_DESIGN.md#L169-L191)

| Initial count | Buffer at ¼ volume | Mean heat (k_BT) | Mean per initial molecule (k_BT) | ln(n+1)/n |
| ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| 4 | 1 | 0.891032 | 0.222758 | 0.402359 |
| 20 | 5 | 1.628482 | 0.081424 | 0.152226 |
| 100 | 25 | 2.419274 | 0.024193 | 0.046151 |
| 400 | 100 | 3.109805 | 0.007775 | 0.014985 |

At engine `2ae9ce8e`, 575 guarded bookings across seven pools matched an independent 88-digit calculation within 1.13 × 10⁻¹² k_BT per heat. Pool volume scaled with the starting tracer count. Exact integer comparisons covered all 42,875 combinations of a matched buffer and withdrawal for 2–50 starting molecules. A complete three-species population wash booked 4.535007 k_BT of heat against a split score of 5.164075. For a mismatched half wash with four tracers, two withdrawn and none supplied, heat exceeds the split score by ln(3/2) k_BT. [Reproducible comparison](checks/matched_buffer.py) · [Engine booking](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/2ae9ce8e5c6b56ecab091b6167d034eb97a5520f/src/cells/models/assembly_lineages_v2/withdrawal.py#L173-L198)

<a id="fr35-withdrawal-energy"></a>

## 21 November 2026 · Energy in a partial pool withdrawal

For any finite ideal pool, any integer split and any fixed buffer at a volume fraction `0 < f < 1`, Lean proves **ΔF = buffer work − pool loss − mixing heat**. The pool loss equals the free energy of the withdrawn sample minus its split score. At each place the score is the negative log binomial probability of the withdrawn count at the physical fraction `f`. The mixing heat has the same formula with the supplied count in place of the withdrawn count, so both are nonnegative in exact arithmetic. The proof uses Mathlib’s normalized binomial distribution and the existing Cells discrete free energy. All energies below are in k_BT. [Lean proof](CellsProofs/Chemistry/PoolWithdrawalEnergy.lean) · [Mathlib binomial mass](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/c44e0c8ee63ca166450922a373c7409c5d26b00b/Mathlib/Probability/ProbabilityMassFunction/Binomial.lean#L25-L44)

With zero supplied tracer, each retained tracer contributes `−ln(1−f)` to the booked heat. For `n` tracers sampled with independent uniform midpoint words, the exact mean is `−n(1−q) ln(1−f)`, where `q` is the fraction of words that trigger withdrawal. For grid fractions such as 1/4 and 1/2, `q=f`. At the largest admissible float, `f=1−2⁻⁵³`, one word in 2⁵² retains the tracer. Its recorded split score is 36.73680057 against a sampler score of 36.04365339, a difference of exactly ln 2. For one tracer, weighting both booked outcomes gives 8.1572 × 10⁻¹⁵ k_BT, exactly twice the continuum mean of 4.0786 × 10⁻¹⁵ k_BT in the model. The finite-word qualification affects interpreting the split score and the design’s mean-heat identity. The per-outcome energy equation holds in both cases. [Finite grid](CellsProofs/Chemistry/PoolWithdrawal.lean) · [Energy booking](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/695f8cdd974e130f25b021df8efeabe4cac8a515/src/cells/models/assembly_lineages_v2/withdrawal.py#L163-L188) · [Design’s mean heat](../docs/ASSEMBLY_WITHDRAWAL_DESIGN.md#L164-L175)

On engine `695f8cdd`, an 88-digit calculation independently checked the five receipt fields in three complete `cells.assembly_lineages@2` worlds and the changes in their saved energy, sink, heat and schedule-work columns. The maximum error there was 1.96 × 10⁻¹⁴ k_BT. Across 1,080 combinations of original, withdrawn and supplied counts and eight fractions, the largest balance error was 6.21 × 10⁻¹⁴ k_BT, within the existing 10⁻¹⁰ k_BT tolerance. All booked heats were nonnegative. In 126 cases a positive reference heat of at most 8.89 × 10⁻¹⁶ k_BT rounded to zero. Four supplied raw words checked which side of a midpoint is taken. Substituting the sample energy for the sink in an eight-molecule control broke closure by ln 32 k_BT. Both existing FR-35 energy tests passed. [Independent comparison](checks/pool_withdrawal_energy.py) · [Host energy columns](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/695f8cdd974e130f25b021df8efeabe4cac8a515/src/cells/models/assembly_lineages_v2/population.py#L785-L825)

The biological comparison still needs the parent count, daughter count and measured daughter volume fraction **for the same split**. Fornell reports 180 passive count pairs at a nominal volume fraction. Liu measured bead retention over 20 splits and volume over 50 droplets without a published pairing. Deshpande’s daughter-size and fluorescence panels cover 152 and 151 pairs respectively. Their single-vesicle movie shows both daughters, but saturated dye intensities prevent calibrated molecule counting. The count studies do not measure the withdrawal’s energy budget. [Count evidence](#fr35-withdrawal) · [Fornell Fig. 2](https://doi.org/10.1063/1.4984131) · [Liu Fig. 4](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6897560/) · [Deshpande Fig. 3](https://ceesdekkerlab.nl/wp-content/uploads/2019/08/Mechanical-division-of-cell-sized-liposomes.pdf#page=5) · [Deshpande Movie S2](https://pmc-oa-opendata.s3.amazonaws.com/PMC5876618.1/nn7b08411_si_003.avi)

<a id="fr35-withdrawal"></a>

## 21 November 2026 · What a partial withdrawal conserves and samples

For every assignment of molecules to the withdrawn sample, the final count plus the withdrawn count equals the initial count plus the fixed buffer. If the pre-draw capacity check passes, every possible final count fits. Under independent sampling with single-molecule probability `q`, the withdrawn count has mean `nq` and variance `nq(1−q)`. The remaining pool after adding `b` molecules has mean `n(1−q)+b` and the same variance. Lean reduces averaging over independent finite words to the Bernoulli recursion already used for lineage fission. [Withdrawal proofs](CellsProofs/Chemistry/PoolWithdrawal.lean) · [Shared moment theorem](CellsProofs/Chemistry/LineageFission.lean#L112)

Cells uses uniform midpoints on a grid with `M = 2^52` bins and withdraws when `u < f`. If the word indices are equiprobable, `q = ceil(M f − 1/2) / M`. The absolute probability error is at most `2^-53`, with equality possible. Fractions `k/M` are exact. At `f = 2^-53` even word zero stays, so no molecules can be withdrawn from any place. The next larger float admits word zero. This sharpens the design’s bound and qualifies the operator docstring’s exact-distribution wording. Independence of keyed Threefry draws is an assumption of the probability calculation. [Accepted words and bound](CellsProofs/Chemistry/PoolWithdrawal.lean#L28) · [Stream conversion](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/4532479c017977c67449995684657066225501d5/src/cells/models/assembly_lineages/streams.py#L120-L128) · [FR-35 design](../docs/ASSEMBLY_WITHDRAWAL_DESIGN.md#L42-L52)

On engine `4532479c`, three checked `cells.assembly_lineages@2` worlds conserved counts at every finite place and kept the slot counts and keys across each wash. Fractions 1/4, 1/2 and 1/3 were tested on the same 4,000 seed addresses with eight molecules per place. The respective observed means were 1.993, 3.991 and 2.6405, and sample variances were 1.479, 1.997 and 1.738. Their predictions were 2, 4 and 2.667 for the means and 1.5, 2 and 1.778 for the variances. Nine boundary cases exercised production masking and thresholding of supplied raw words. Three existing FR-35 tests passed, including the independent booking reference. [Comparison and replays](checks/pool_withdrawal.py) · [Host withdrawal](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/4532479c017977c67449995684657066225501d5/src/cells/models/assembly_lineages_v2/population.py#L785-L825)

Search and the comparison driver independently recovered all 180 passive-control droplets in Fornell et al. from the vector markers. Assigning the center daughter the nominal volume fraction 1/3 predicts a mean bead fraction of 0.333. The observed mean of the individual fractions was **0.4501**. Pooling beads across droplets gives a different quantity, **2010/4269 = 0.4708**. At fixed parent counts of 7, 9 and 12, the sample variances were **0.95, 2.18 and 4.48 beads²** in groups of 7, 12 and 7 splits. The predictions were **1.56, 2.00 and 2.67 beads²**. Individual daughter volumes were unreported, and fluid motion could bias particle collection. This comparison tests the nominal-volume assumption for 10 µm beads. [Fornell et al., Fig. 2c](https://doi.org/10.1063/1.4984131) · [Vector PDF, p. 3](https://pmc-oa-opendata.s3.amazonaws.com/PMC5446280.1/PMC5446280.1.pdf#page=3) · [Digitized counts](checks/data/pool_withdrawal_measurements.json)

Liu et al. measured passive retention after withdrawing liquid through side channels at 4.5 µL/min. The retained center volume of 12.8 ± 1.1 nL divided by the approximately 47 nL parent at the same flow gives **27.2%** predicted bead retention. They observed **28.2% ± 4.4 percentage points SD** over 20 splits. Volumes used 50 droplets. This geometry supplies a comparison of means. Counts and volumes paired within each split would test whether each particle has the measured liquid withdrawal probability. [Liu et al., Fig. 4b–c](https://doi.org/10.1063/1.5129256) · [Inputs and uncertainty](checks/data/pool_withdrawal_measurements.json)

<a id="binding-margin-vertices"></a>

## 21 November 2026 · Finite vertex tests for binding equilibria

For one held ligand, the only normalized weight is 1. For two, write the weights as `(t, 1 − t)`. Each form’s signed ligand count then projects to a line. Between consecutive crossings, the same form stays lowest for each binder and the joint margin is affine. Its minimum is attained at an endpoint or a rational same-binder crossing in the unit interval. Lean proves that checking these finitely many directions is necessary and sufficient for a positive equilibrium, including with signed counts, tied forms and zero binder totals. [Vertex theorems](CellsProofs/Chemistry/BindingVertices.lean) · [Equilibrium condition](claims/binding-equilibrium-feasibility.md) · [Production enumeration](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/e22fc9afc2ca68ea2e6a2950b70f3a423cadc61b/src/cells/laws/reactions/rapid_binding.py#L279-L324)

In any number of held ligands, the simplex corners suffice when **each binder has one form that attains that binder’s lowest count in every held coordinate**. Subtract that form’s contribution from each ligand total. The margin is the weighted sum of those remainders and its minimum is their smallest value. The one- and two-ligand theorem covers S1–S5 and S7, comprising 69 registered states. In the three-ligand S6 chemistry, both binders have zero-count base forms and only nonnegative counts. Its three corners therefore suffice for every set of totals, including all 11 registered S6 states. The production enumeration returned 17 directions for S6. [Common-floor proof](CellsProofs/Chemistry/BindingVertices.lean#L219) · [Registered form counts](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/e22fc9afc2ca68ea2e6a2950b70f3a423cadc61b/validation/core_seams/references/reference_inputs.json)

On engines `e22fc9af` and `b521a11c`, all 80 registered inventories matched their frozen rational minimum and recorded minimizing direction. On all 236 nonempty held-ligand projections, NumPy and compiled JAX CPU returned the rounded exact margin. The driver independently intersects the count lines for two ligands and uses rational plane intersections for three. Its `--emit-lean` option writes 31 fixture checks, binding the listed directions and common-floor hypotheses to the original form counts. Lean checked them all. Ten existing engine tests passed, including the C4 challenges and the caps on enumeration. [Comparison driver and fixture generator](checks/binding_vertices.py) · [Frozen reference values](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/e22fc9afc2ca68ea2e6a2950b70f3a423cadc61b/validation/core_seams/references/designs/core_seams_reference_values.json)

Two controls show why the hypotheses matter. Of the candidate vertices in the two-ligand fixture, only (1/3, 2/3) gives a negative margin, −1/24 mM for 1 mM binder. Omitting it gives +5/8 mM. In a three-ligand fixture that can release any one of three ligands, the minimum is −1/24 mM at (1/3, 1/3, 1/3). The next smallest listed value is +1/8 mM and every corner gives +5/8 mM. Both CPU backends identified both negative margins. General completeness of the three-ligand vertex enumeration with incomparable forms remains open. Float64 sign decisions close to zero retain their earlier failure record. [Omitted-vertex controls](checks/binding_vertices.py#L131) · [Signed-total failures](claims/binding-equilibrium-feasibility.md#numerical-comparison)

<a id="binding-equilibrium-feasibility"></a>

## 21 November 2026 · Which totals admit a binding equilibrium?

A finite binding ensemble with nonnegative binder totals has exactly one positive equilibrium if and only if every normalized nonnegative weighting of the held ligands leaves a positive inventory above the binders’ lowest projected counts. An available solution leaves at least the smallest free concentration in every weighting. Conversely, a positive minimum margin bounds the low-energy states of the binding potential in a finite box. At its minimum every held inventory residual vanishes. Fixed unheld activities enter only in the form weights. When every binder has a zero-count base form, positive held totals guarantee feasibility. Finite vertex enumeration and the fixed Newton iteration budget remain numerical obligations. [Five Lean theorems](CellsProofs/Chemistry/BindingFeasibility.lean) · [Uniqueness](CellsProofs/Chemistry/BindingEquilibrium.lean) · [Engine equations](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/0310d9f0cfc758181c0ef145bb09d513f1ef69ec/src/cells/laws/reactions/rapid_binding.py#L12-L39)

The comparison allocates nonnegative concentrations to each present form from the original species registry, conserves every binder total, and uses a linear program to maximize the common free remainder. NumPy and compiled JAX CPU matched its margin on all 236 nonempty held-coordinate projections of the 80 registered S1–S7 states. All 80 empty projections returned positive infinity. The four C4 challenges gave the correct margins and solver verdicts. Fifteen existing engine tests passed. [Comparison and independent allocation](checks/binding_feasibility.py) · [C4 reference states](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/0310d9f0cfc758181c0ef145bb09d513f1ef69ec/tests/unit/test_rapid_binding_kernel.py#L189-L265)

### Signed proton totals at float64 resolution

For copies of the registry’s `rel1` binder, the two forms hold zero or minus one proton equivalent. The exact margin is the signed H total plus the sum of binder totals. All entries below are in mM. The reference uses exact fractions of the supplied binary64 values. The admissions shown are the actual `free_ligands` verdicts with its residual check and margin enabled. [Reproducer](checks/binding_feasibility.py) · [Margin calculation and strict comparison](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/0310d9f0cfc758181c0ef145bb09d513f1ef69ec/src/cells/laws/reactions/rapid_binding.py#L244-L277)

| H total | Binder totals | Exact margin | NumPy margin and verdict | Compiled JAX CPU margin and verdict |
| ---: | --- | ---: | --- | --- |
| −1.7 | 0.5, 0.6, 0.6 | 0 | +2.22 × 10⁻¹⁶, admitted | +2.22 × 10⁻¹⁶, admitted |
| −0.8999999999999999 | 0.3, 0.3, 0.3 | +2⁻⁵⁴ | 0, refused | 0, refused |
| −3.8000000000000003 | 0.3, 0.3, 1.0, 1.1, 1.1 | −2⁻⁵³ | +4.44 × 10⁻¹⁶, admitted | 0, refused |

The residual-only check passed all three examples on both backends. A fixed absolute cutoff would also discard genuinely positive margins. The relevant lead decision is how to resolve the sign when its rounding interval includes zero before admitting signed held totals. The `resolved_pump_tissue@3` parser accepted its ordinary S7 control and rejected zero and −1.7 mM initial H rows. The kernel finding concerns that prospective signed-total domain. The comparison exits with status 1 while the classifications disagree with the exact reference. [Tissue host domain](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/0310d9f0cfc758181c0ef145bb09d513f1ef69ec/src/cells/kinds/proton_buffer.py#L83-L88) · [Boundary and parser checks](checks/binding_feasibility.py)

Cortes et al. observed an absorbance plateau near 0.39 at 705 nm as Fe(II) added to 40 µM Par increased from about 20 to 32 µM at pH 7.1. The trace illustrates a finite binding capacity. The authors also used the 1:2 Fe-to-ligand breakpoint to calibrate the ligand stock. Residual free Fe, endpoint errors and replicate counts are unavailable for this panel, so the strict mathematical boundary has no independent experimental test here. [Supporting Fig. S3b](https://s3-eu-west-1.amazonaws.com/pstorage-acs-6854636/8172722/ic7b00372_si_001.pdf#page=14) · [Calibration method](https://s3-eu-west-1.amazonaws.com/pstorage-acs-6854636/8172722/ic7b00372_si_001.pdf#page=4) · [Measurement conditions](checks/data/binding_feasibility_measurements.json)

<a id="binding-equilibrium-unique"></a>

## 21 November 2026 · One binding equilibrium for prescribed totals

At fixed temperature, standard potentials and nonnegative binder totals, the ligand inventories determine at most one positive free concentration vector. The proof follows a line on which several free log concentrations change together. Its derivative is the full response matrix applied to that change. Positive definiteness makes the projected inventory increase strictly along every nonconstant line. The result also covers fixed unheld ligands, signed counts, empty binders and any measured group of forms. Existence of a solution for a proposed set of totals and convergence within a fixed Newton budget are separate questions. [Lean proof](CellsProofs/Chemistry/BindingEquilibrium.lean) · [Engine equations](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/0310d9f0cfc758181c0ef145bb09d513f1ef69ec/src/cells/laws/reactions/rapid_binding.py#L100-L142)

On public engine `0310d9f0`, NumPy and compiled JAX CPU gave a positive projected change for all 423 pairs of the 80 S1–S7 reference states, holding each binder inventory fixed across each pair. In 163 pairs, different free ligands moved in opposite directions. An independent partition calculation used the original standard potentials and the symmetrized relative entropy of each binder’s form fractions. Dropping the bound contribution was detectable in all 423 pairs. The public B4 and Newton paths each ran on their registered chemistries and recovered all 423 independently constructed target states per backend. The largest error was 2.99 × 10⁻¹³ in log concentration. Ten existing engine tests passed, including the two-ligand tissue run. [Comparison driver](checks/binding_equilibrium.py) · [Reference inputs](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/0310d9f0cfc758181c0ef145bb09d513f1ef69ec/validation/core_seams/references/reference_inputs.json)

Fixing selected coordinates gave 284 distinct pairs and 139 identical endpoints. Of the distinct pairs, 283 were separated on both backends. For S4 states 10 and 12 with H fixed, free Mg changed from 0.49999999999999994 to 0.5000000000000001 mM. Both paths returned 5.881688425907021 mM total Mg at both ends. An 80-digit evaluation at the stored log inputs gave a positive projection of 1.79 × 10⁻³¹ mM. The nearest binary64 reference totals differ by one unit in the last place. The comparison retains this tie and the independent error bound for every pair. [Rounded-pair calculation](checks/binding_equilibrium.py)

Cortes et al. prepared the same totals of 30.8 µM Fe(II), 72 µM Tar and 220 µM EGTA at pH 7.2, starting with iron bound to either Tar or EGTA. The measured Fe–Tar complex started near 31 or 0 µM and reached about 22.5 µM in both preparations by 15–20 min, with traces close through 90 min. Uniqueness predicts a zero equilibrium difference without an affinity fit. The figure resolves a difference only to about ±1 µM across the two readings and supplies no endpoint sampling error. The absorbance pools Fe–Tar protonation states. Free iron and individual microscopic forms would require separate measurements. [Cortes et al., Fig. S4a, p. S-15](https://s3-eu-west-1.amazonaws.com/pstorage-acs-6854636/8172722/ic7b00372_si_001.pdf#page=15) · [Measurement conditions](checks/data/binding_equilibrium_measurements.json)

For the earlier H–Mg response claim, Spicer observed a fall in pH on adding magnesium salt to 2.5 mM ATP. This supports acidification in that preparation. Quantifying proton release at fixed pH would require measured free Mg and proton equivalents. [Spicer, Fig. 2, p. 304](https://doi.org/10.1016/S0021-9258(18)44837-5) · [Conditions and measurement limits](checks/data/binding_equilibrium_measurements.json)

<a id="binding-ligand-response"></a>

## 21 November 2026 · How total ligand responds to free concentration

For `rapid_binding@1`, increasing one free ligand at fixed binder totals and other free concentrations strictly increases that ligand’s total. The derivative with respect to its log concentration is its free concentration plus a sum of variances weighted by binder totals. Counts may be signed, so the result includes forms that release a proton on binding Mg. Every cross-derivative is a corresponding covariance. The full matrix is positive definite, including the engine’s identity on unheld coordinates. The proof imports Mathlib’s results on positive diagonal matrices, weighted matrix products and derivatives. [Lean proof](CellsProofs/Chemistry/BindingResponse.lean) · [Engine residual and response matrix](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/68ab12e7f57552fa551b103df1393a1e095cd001/src/cells/laws/reactions/rapid_binding.py#L122-L142)

At engine `68ab12e7`, 303 entries across 80 S1–S7 states were compared with an 80-digit calculation from the original standard potentials. NumPy, compiled JAX CPU and automatic differentiation of the conservation residual agreed within 3.49 × 10⁻¹⁵ of each entry’s uncentered absolute terms (maximum absolute discrepancy 2.45 × 10⁻¹⁴ mM). All 80 matrices were positive definite on each path. All nine S7 states had a negative H–Mg entry and a positive Mg diagonal. The tests also exercised unheld coordinates and empty binders. Removing binder covariance was detected in every system. Nine existing engine tests passed, including central differences of the inverse sensitivity and an H/Mg tissue run with `resolved_pump_tissue@3`. [Comparison driver](checks/binding_response.py) · [Engine sensitivity test](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/68ab12e7f57552fa551b103df1393a1e095cd001/tests/unit/test_rapid_binding_kernel.py#L476) · [Two-ligand tissue test](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/68ab12e7f57552fa551b103df1393a1e095cd001/tests/unit/test_rapid_binding_world_two_ligands.py#L31)

In a 3 mM ATP solution at pH 7.2 and 37 °C, two points from Ward et al. show Mg-bound ATP rising from about 80.8% to 89.7% as measured free Mg rose from about 130 to 230 µM. Using Zhang et al.’s independently measured apparent affinity, Cells predicts 59.8% to 72.5%. The measurements support the direction. Transferring the affinity between preparations overpredicts the increase by 3.7 percentage points and underpredicts both individual occupancies. The figure gives neither point-specific sampling errors nor total Mg for the two markers. The quantitative total-Mg slope and the H–Mg cross-response remain open experimentally. [Marker readings and conditions](checks/data/ward1998_mgatp_titration.json) · [Ward et al., Fig. 1](https://cds.ismrm.org/ismrm-1998/PDF6/p1693.pdf) · [Zhang et al.](https://doi.org/10.1006/abio.1997.2238)

## 21 November 2026 · Finite binding forms at fixed ligand activities

Physlib’s finite counting ensemble gives each `rapid_binding@1` form probability when its Boltzmann exponent equals the Cells log weight up to a common offset. The shared Cells source proves the change from standard concentration to mM, max-shifted normalization, and how to combine equal-count isomers at any fixed ligand chemical potentials. Equal-energy multiplicity enters once. Seven local theorems compile on Lean 4.19.0. The optional Lean 4.22.0 check uses the same source and the existing Physlib pin. Its two theorems use the standard Lean axioms and Physlib’s positive Boltzmann-constant axiom. [Cells proofs](CellsProofs/Chemistry/BindingEnsemble.lean) · [Physlib comparison](checks/physlib/BindingEnsemble.lean) · [Reproduction](README.md#reusing-a-proof)

On engine `d0104950`, NumPy and JIT-compiled CPU float64 each evaluated 536 present form fractions across 80 manufactured states in S1–S7. The largest absolute difference from the 80-digit partition calculated from the original standard potentials was 7.78 × 10⁻¹⁶ or less. All 52 padded entries were zero on each backend. From the initialized two-ligand `resolved_pump_tissue@3` stage read, all six S3 form concentrations agreed with the existing reference within 8.8% of each form’s numerical tolerance. In a synthetic example with one free state and two equal-weight bound isomers, the occupied fraction was 2/3. Omitting one isomer gave 1/2 and counting the pair twice gave 4/5. The registry refused two separate declarations with identical ligand counts. [Comparison](checks/binding_ensemble.py) · [Registry convention](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/d0104950c40b92860a211c9e2555709d0fd61aed/src/cells/registry/species_registry.py#L399-L405)

For Mg–ATP at pH 7.2 and 37 °C, Zhang et al. measured an apparent Kd of 87.4 ± 4.9 µM (SD, four measurements). Using that value at 230 µM free Mg gives a bound fraction of 0.725 in the two-form Cells projection. Ward et al.’s independent MOPS/KCl preparation gives about 0.897 at that concentration from the endpoint of their Figure 1. The difference is 0.172 in fractional occupancy. The preparations and measurement methods differ. The measurements constrain aggregate Mg-bound ATP and leave the individual H/Mg forms and their multiplicities open. [Zhang et al.](https://doi.org/10.1006/abio.1997.2238) · [Ward et al., Fig. 1](https://cds.ismrm.org/ismrm-1998/PDF6/p1693.pdf) · [Measurement inputs and uncertainty](checks/data/mgatp_binding.json)

<a id="kv15-ph-activation"></a>

## 21 November 2026 · Kv1.5 activation and the current retained at pH 6.8

Trapani and Korn measured wild-type human Kv1.5 in HEK293 cells with 100 mM internal and 10 mM external potassium. At 0 mV, Figure 1F gives peak tail conductances of about 0.915 at pH 7.3 and 0.66 at pH 6.8. Each curve uses its own maximum. If both maxima are equal and the peak openings transfer between protocols, their ratio predicts **72.1%** retained step current. Figure 1B, reserved for evaluation, reports **74.5 ± 1.9%** (SEM, eight cells) in a 75 ms pulse from −80 to 0 mV. The prediction is 2.37 percentage points below the measurement. Reading each marker within ±0.01 gives 70.3–74.0% from the graph. A statistical comparison would also need uncertainty in both curve readings, their covariance and the ratio of absolute maxima. [Original Figure 1B and 1F](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC1302603/#fig1) · [Measurements, protocols and graph coordinates](checks/data/trapani2003_kv15_ph.json)

The registered Kv1.5 activation midpoint is −6.0 mV. The paper gives −15.3 mV in control and −3.6 mV in acid. Applying the measured +11.7 mV shift to the registered gate gives **40.5%** retained peak current. Supplying both measured midpoints with the registered slope and clocks gives **69.5%**. The voltage-only law run under both pH labels gives 100%. These comparisons keep inactivation kinetics, channel count, permeability and ionic drive fixed. Lean proves how the ratio of fully open currents enters the prediction, and that a right shift of a rising Boltzmann curve of fixed width gives a strictly increasing treated/control activation ratio with voltage. The paper does not give panel-specific slopes. A comparison of absolute maximal conductance and the control activation curve in this preparation would distinguish the remaining explanations. [Lean theorems](CellsProofs/Transport/IonChannels.lean#L298) · [Engine comparison](checks/kv15_ph.py)

<a id="kv15-channel-density"></a>

## 21 November 2026 · Kv1.5 availability at an intermediate density

In tagged Kv1.5 expressed in HEK293 cells, the conducting fraction read from Figure 8C is about 0.34, 0.23 and 0.18 at 115, 300 and 425 voltage-sensing channels/µm². The two endpoints determine a one-site inhibition curve `A K / (K + density)` with a maximum of 0.507 and a half-saturation density of 234 channels/µm². The middle point was excluded from calibration. The curve predicts 0.222 there. Holding the low-density fraction constant predicts 0.34. The middle fraction is a three-cell moving average from the same figure. The authors give no pointwise errors, and gating count enters the two axes in opposite directions. At comparable densities, two Kv1.4 markers give about 0.64 and 0.73. These measurements leave the mechanism and its transfer to other channels open. [Published figure, p. 766](https://ars.els-cdn.com/content/image/1-s2.0-S0006349522000716-mmc3.pdf#page=12) · [Measurements and reading convention](checks/data/maverick2022_kv_density.json)

The existing composite reproduced 0.222 on a separate reporter through its population-density route, with Hill exponent one, basal zero and the fitted schedule. The parser enforces D218 when the source and target are the same channel population. For Kv1.5 itself, three synthetic patches of 100, 160 and 240 µm² ran through the FR-34 clamp at −120 and +60 mV. After division by channel count, fixed availability gave ratios of one. Supplying the three measured fractions gave ratios of 1, 0.6765 and 0.5294 on NumPy and compiled JAX CPU. Lean proves this cancellation for unequal patch areas given shared gates, permeability, concentrations, voltage and reference density, with a nonzero reference current. The optional grn-lean check gives the affine reciprocal used for the intermediate prediction. [Count-normalized current](CellsProofs/Transport/IonChannels.lean#L266) · [Upstream Hill specialization](checks/hill/Response.lean#L60) · [Comparison driver](checks/channel_density.py) · [D218 self-source rule](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/1b8017d13755bed2ee9be09375c998e364a4ca62/src/cells/operators/species_regulated_availability.py#L598-L607)

<a id="fr34-clamped-current"></a>

## 21 November 2026 · What an instantaneous channel current identifies

For `resolved_pump_tissue@3`, a declared voltage changes the driving force while the saved gate values stay fixed. Summing the outward species fluxes with their signed charges gives the population current. At a fixed area and reference density, abundance is proportional to the number of channels. Lean proves that any measured voltage set containing a nonzero open-channel response identifies the product of abundance and availability when the gates and drive are known. Two preparations with the same product give the same curve. The proof uses Mathlib’s finite-sum theorem. The optional ATOMS Lab check gives amperes for a molar flux times a Faraday conversion. [Current proof](CellsProofs/Transport/IonChannels.lean#L226) · [Dimensional proof](checks/units/FacePower.lean#L31)

On the frozen engine `1b8017d1`, NumPy and compiled JAX CPU evaluated 11 channel populations on two patches at six voltage pairs. The largest current difference from the 70-digit constant-field reference was 1.30 × 10⁻²⁶ A or less on each backend. Halving either the declared abundance or the held availability halved every flux and current exactly in these fixtures. Full block and the padded population gave zero. On the chloride patch the outward-positive current was −17.6623 pA at −120 mV and +6.7983 pA at +40 mV. The existing host test also passed for routed availability at a declared voltage. [Engine readout](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/1b8017d13755bed2ee9be09375c998e364a4ca62/src/cells/models/surface_pump_v3.py#L405-L448) · [Comparison](checks/clamped_channel_current.py) · [Host test](https://github.com/Eidosoma/Cells/blob/1b8017d13755bed2ee9be09375c998e364a4ca62/tests/unit/test_surface_pump_v3_populations.py#L165)

Maverick and Tamkun estimated the conducting fraction independently of simply counting channels at the membrane. In HEK293 cells the mean fractions were 0.96 ± 0.05 for Kv1.4 and 0.26 ± 0.01 for Kv1.5 (SEM). For Kv1.5, the three-cell moving average fell from about 0.34 at 115 to 0.18 at 425 voltage-sensing channels/µm². Holding availability at the low-density value would predict 0.34 at the high-density endpoint. The plotted points come from different cells and carry no pointwise error bars. This is a target for a density-dependent availability model. Absolute current at a specified voltage requires a matched measurement. [Figure 8B–C, p. 766](https://ars.els-cdn.com/content/image/1-s2.0-S0006349522000716-mmc3.pdf#page=12) · [Primary paper](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.bpj.2022.01.021) · [Data and interpretation](checks/data/maverick2022_kv_density.json)

## 21 November 2026 · Reusing public physics proofs

The cleft and the scheduled compartment wash use the same ideal mixing term. The wash proof now reuses the cleft’s Mathlib-based sign bound for each exchanged neutral species and proves zero mixing heat at complete replacement. On Lean 4.19.0 the optional comparison with Mathlib’s `klFun` also proves that the term vanishes exactly at a concentration ratio of one. Adding that import to the default library would have raised its transitive Mathlib source-module count from 1,725 to 2,079. The optional ATOMS Lab check on the same toolchain verifies the units of charge × voltage for electrode work. [Wash heat](claims/compartment-wash.md) · [Relative entropy](checks/mixing/KLFun.lean) · [Electrode units](checks/units/FacePower.lean)

With Physlib v4.22.0, Lean recovered the single gate’s open probability `1 / (1 + exp u)` from the two-state partition function under the identification `u = β E`. Physlib postulates a positive Boltzmann constant in the chosen units. The completed specialization uses that postulate and Lean’s `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`. The experiment leaves the fitted voltage slope, kinetic rates and Cx43 measurements as separate questions. ## 21 November 2026 · Five repairs and the new registered mechanisms

The review used clean CPU snapshots of Cells at `d74f519d` and `a6235d7b`. The second includes the midpoint repair. Comparing the cited files of the previous 74 claims with `a69f38ab` flagged 61 of 87 comparison records across 54 claims. Sixty records referenced a changed engine source file. <a id="nov21-features"></a>

### Registered laws, operators and the two-gate junction

- **FR-21 prescribed shell.** Lean proves that equal permeability × prescribed area gives equal entry and exit rates at fixed activities. It also proves that counts outside the declared gate forms leave the factor unchanged. Two existing Cells tests passed, including comparison with the closed-form propensity, variation of wall/site counts, and single-lineage and population run records. [Proof](CellsProofs/Chemistry/PrescribedShell.lean) · [Engine comparisons](../tests/unit/test_assembly_lineages_v2_geometry.py#L538)
- **FR-26 replacement of occupied sites and FR-24 site readouts.** Lean accounts for departing bound ligand and the incoming template occupancy times capacity. In the compiled replacement world the ligand and product balances stayed within 10⁻¹⁵ fmol. Omitting the departing ligand produced the expected deficit. Four targeted operator tests passed for site quantities, the post-replacement read, channel control and midpoint evaluation. [Proof](CellsProofs/Tissue/CellReplacement.lean) · [Replacement comparison](../tests/unit/test_site_replacement_runs.py#L101) · [Site readouts](../tests/unit/test_species_regulated_availability.py)
- **FR-30 Cx43 series gates.** The compiled functions matched the independent four-term conductance expansion, the ion route and a zero-residual tracer route to within 2.23 × 10⁻¹⁶ absolute error across 120 clamp samples and all four gate-kind pairings. At this revision the functions are executable and await host registration. The mixed amplitude still needs a measurement in a single biological trace. [Driver](checks/junction_series_gates.py) · [Amplitude claim](claims/junction-series-amplitudes.md)
- **FR-35 stochastic partial withdrawal.** Decision D261 specifies a separate assembly event with per-place draws. Its design is available. A registry entry and executable event are still pending at the reviewed head. [Withdrawal design](../docs/ASSEMBLY_WITHDRAWAL_DESIGN.md)

<a id="nov21-wash"></a>

### FR-27 finite-compartment wash and experimental dilution

Four Lean theorems cover inventory, positive free amount, whole replacement and repeated dilution toward a fixed buffer. The compiled event with 60 fmol of free tracer retained both site populations and all ions through four test schedules. The largest discrepancy in free amount, collected amount, supplied amount or balance was 7.11 × 10⁻¹⁵ fmol. Whole-wash tissue runs starting with 64 fmol reached 32 fmol and 64 × 2⁻⁵⁰ fmol exactly. The run targeting 64 × 2⁻⁶⁰ fmol refused its first wash in keeping with the documented resolution of the host's amount representation. [Proof](CellsProofs/Tissue/CompartmentWash.lean) · [Driver](checks/compartment_wash.py) · [Engine representation limit](../src/cells/operators/wash_schedule.py#L43)

Vloemans et al. measured free fluorescein in successive 10 µL plugs. Their one-step retained fractions were 0.498, 0.193 and 0.093 for expected fractions of 0.50, 0.20 and 0.10. Starting the three 80% exchanges at the measured 1.712 µg/mL gives ideal values of 0.3424, 0.06848 and 0.013696 µg/mL. The printed means were 0.332, 0.067 and 0.020 µg/mL. The last mean is 46% above the ideal value. These data address freely dissolved dye. The study reports graphical SDs, possible wall adsorption and a limited instrument range. Bound-site retention and mixing heat in a biological well remain open. [Figure 4](https://doi.org/10.1039/D4LC00195H) · [Measured values](checks/data/vloemans2024_dilution.json) · [Comparison](checks/compartment_wash.py)

<a id="pool-replacement"></a>

## 20 November 2026 · Tracer and energy at a finite-pool wash

At an isolated `cells.pool_replacement@1` stop, the fresh pool enters, the spent pool leaves and each living assembly retains its contents. Lean proves the per-species inventory, the inventory of a conserved tracer through subsequent exchanges, and the free-energy change when the finite bath and retained assemblies have additive energies. Work and the sink entry can each be signed. [Proofs](CellsProofs/Chemistry/VesiclePopulation.lean#L127) · [Registered operator](../src/cells/models/assembly_lineages_v2/replacement.py#L98)

The CPU `cells.assembly_lineages@2` comparison at engine `a69f38ab` ran two vesicles. The pulse supplied 12 tracer molecules at 1 s. At 2 s the wash removed the six in the bath, keeping six in the vesicles, including three in bound complexes. The first release into the fresh bath occurred at 2.000848 s and the first subsequent uptake at 2.001470 s. Each slot and key matched immediately across both stops, and the tracer inventory closed in integer counts at all 856 saved rows. The reread relations bundle carried all six nonzero material edges at the two stops. Both independent bath energies, their schedule entries and the change in total free energy agreed within 2.85 × 10⁻¹⁴ kBT. [CPU comparison](checks/pool_replacement.py) · [Finite-pool record](../src/cells/models/assembly_lineages_v2/records.py#L511)

Zhao et al. washed trapped giant vesicles with inflow free of fluorescent dextran. After 40 min at 1 µL/min, about ten chamber volumes, exterior signal was approximately 0.1 fluorescence units. Surviving vesicles retained fluorescent contents, and some vesicles were lost. This supports the spatial separation of a wash endpoint. Its duration and the fraction of cargo retained matter when comparing an actual wash with the instantaneous model. Sumi et al. measured redistribution of a fluorescent lipid between donor and acceptor liposomes. With twice as much acceptor material, the signal reached about 48% of total fluorescence at 3.8 min. The Cells fixture uses synthetic tracer kinetics. These experiments give neither its return time nor the identity of the recaptured molecules. [Zhao et al., Fig. S3a–c](https://www.mpikg.mpg.de/th/people/dimova/publications/Zhao%20AdvMat%2021.pdf#page=17) · [Sumi et al., Fig. 7A](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5460167/#Fig7) · [Measurements and uncertainty](checks/data/pool_replacement_measurements.json)

<a id="pool-replacement-capacity"></a>

**An accepted fresh pool can have incorrect work.** With `molecule_capacity=32`, a fresh bath containing 33 molecules of one finite species passed admission. Both pool replacement and serial transfer completed, saved all 33 molecules, and passed their internal energy check. Each booked **−53.676961 kBT**. The discrete ideal-mixture energy is **−50.180454 kBT**. Changing only capacity to 64 recovered the reference; 32 molecules at capacity 32 also passed. The accepted bath was below the osmotic limit. [Capacity cases](checks/pool_replacement.py) · [Ideal bath energy](../src/cells/models/assembly_lineages_v2/ideal_mixing.py#L64)

The energy calculation clipped the log-factorial index to 32 while its linear term used 33 molecules. Lean proves that the first count beyond capacity misses exactly **ln(capacity + 1)**, here **3.496508 kBT**. The ledger used the same clipped energy on both sides and could still close. The assembly owner can check each declared fresh count against the execution capacity for both schedule kinds before applying a stop. A count equal to capacity is valid. The owner can also align the operator's published trigger text with the implemented refusal of simultaneous schedules. [Gap theorem](CellsProofs/Chemistry/VesiclePopulation.lean#L153) · [Clipped table lookup](../src/cells/models/assembly_lineages_v2/mass_action.py#L66) · [Published trigger](../src/cells/models/assembly_lineages_v2/replacement.py#L88) · [Schedule admission](../src/cells/models/assembly_lineages_v2/division.py#L165)

<a id="mitochondrial-kinase-supply"></a>

## 20 November 2026 · Adenylate kinase and the held reductant supply

**Adenylate kinase.** One signed extent of 2 ADP ⇌ ATP + AMP preserves adenylates, phosphate groups and base-form charge. Lean proves that the rate evaluated on Mg-free nucleotides equals the rate written on total nucleotides when ATP and ADP each bind one Mg and AMP stays free. For Mg dissociation constants of 0.024 mM (ATP) and 0.347 mM (ADP), the apparent equilibrium constant on totals peaks at **1.5104 at 0.299 mM free Mg**. The proof gives the exact drop from that maximum at any other nonnegative free Mg. The declared buffer match gives 0.4331 at 3.80855 mM. Omitting the Mg prefactor preserves the zero-flux point but slows the test flux by a factor of **159.69** there. [Engine declaration](../src/cells/laws/cycles/adenylate_kinase.py) · [Proof](CellsProofs/Chemistry/MitochondrialReactions.lean#L17) · [Buffer match](../docs/MITOCHONDRIA_LAWS_APPENDIX.md#L416)

Two enzyme assays compare the same total-nucleotide quotient. At pH 7.0 and 38 °C Lawson and Veech reported **0.391 and 1.05** at 0 and 1 mM free Mg. Cells gives **0.3890 and 1.1013** with its 25 °C parameters. Zero Mg is the formula's limit. In purified maize-leaf enzyme at 1.8–1.9 mM free Mg, the reported quotient was **0.76 ± 0.10**, and Cells gives **0.744–0.772**. The assays support the dependence on Mg. Cardiac intermembrane-space calibration and the predicted peak position remain open. [Lawson and Veech](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36398/) · [Kleczkowski and Randall](https://doi.org/10.1093/jxb/42.4.537) · [Values and uncertainty](checks/data/mitochondrial_reaction_measurements.json)

**Held reductant supply.** At stall the free matrix NADH/NAD ratio times free proton concentration equals the held reduced-to-oxidized donor ratio times its reference proton concentration. With the declared donor ratio, this gives **4.5807 at pH 7.1** and **9.1397 at pH 7.4**, at either phosphate concentration tested. Lean also proves that the work supplied by the held pair equals heat plus chemical work delivered to NAD, NADH and H⁺. Reversing the flux changed the sign of supplied work while heat stayed positive. The energy balance closed in both directions to a relative tolerance of 2 × 10⁻¹². [Engine declaration](../src/cells/laws/cycles/held_reductant_supply.py) · [Stall and work proofs](CellsProofs/Chemistry/MitochondrialReactions.lean#L73)

Williamson, Lund and Krebs measured a real donor pair, β-hydroxybutyrate and acetoacetate. At 38 °C the equilibrium quotient including one proton was **(4.94 ± 0.086) × 10⁻⁹ M** (SEM, 25 determinations over pH 6.84–7.16). This supplies evidence for the proton dependence. Its equilibrium constant belongs to that named pair. Cells assigns the standard free energy of its own donor, and its absolute stall ratio and heat remain experimentally open. [Williamson et al., Table 3](https://doi.org/10.1042/bj1030514) · [Recorded assay](checks/data/mitochondrial_reaction_measurements.json)

**Engine replay.** The registered rate evaluator at `a69f38ab` passed 21 kinase cases and 24 supply cases spanning both directions and stall. Maximum absolute flux errors divided by the forward rate were **7.03 × 10⁻¹⁵** and **1.22 × 10⁻¹⁴**, respectively. In a separate two-step CPU `resolved_pump_tissue@3` run, stored ATP, ADP, AMP, NAD and NADH changes matched the signed extents. The held rows stayed fixed and their supply records carried opposite signs. The earlier seven-cycle comparisons now select their seven populations explicitly because the shared fixture defaults to nine. The two-step material check and the NumPy and compiled JAX stall checks passed again on that selection. [Two-reaction driver](checks/mitochondrial_reactions.py) · [Seven-cycle material check](checks/mitochondrial_cycles.py) · [Seven-cycle stall check](checks/mitochondrial_stall.py)

<a id="cx43-series-gates"></a>

## 20 November 2026 · What a Cx43 junction must predict at opposite voltages

The current `junction_voltage_relaxation@1` on `resolved_pump_tissue@3` uses voltage magnitude. At −80 and +80 mV its compiled intrinsic steady conductance was **0.1003927063 at both voltages** with the reference parameters. Its tissue run also gave identical gate values at both polarities after 31.25 ms. Lean proves that a shared prediction must miss at least one of two targets by half their separation. [Engine rates](../src/cells/laws/junctions/junction_voltage_relaxation.py#L41) · [Proof](CellsProofs/Transport/JunctionGate.lean#L103) · [Comparison driver](checks/junction_gate.py)

Revilla, Castro and Barrio measured wild-type rat Cx43 in paired Xenopus oocytes. Their two published Boltzmann fits give **0.487445 at −80 mV** and **0.381711 at +80 mV**, on the same prepulse-normalized scale. Any polarity-symmetric gate therefore misses at least one fitted curve by about **0.05287** at 80 mV, even after refitting its parameters. This bound concerns the rounded central fits. Table 1 gives no parameter uncertainties or covariance for a significance test. Figure 3C reports measured means and SEM. [Table 1 and Fig. 3](https://doi.org/10.1016/S0006-3495(99)76986-9) · [Fit parameters and units](checks/data/revilla1999_cx43.json)

**A separate prediction for FR-30.** Its proposed gate multiplies independent fast and slow factors. At a fixed voltage the product has a plateau and three transient terms. The third decays at the sum of the two rates. Lean proves that the plateau times this third amplitude equals the fast amplitude times the slow amplitude. A common multiplicative normalization preserves the equality. If the two fitted times of 70 and 931 ms from the N2a study are used as gate times, the third time is **65.1 ms**. Distinguishing that from 70 ms requires the waveform and enough precision to resolve the two terms. [Design](../docs/JUNCTION_TWO_RELAXATION_DESIGN.md#L174) · [Amplitude proof](CellsProofs/Transport/JunctionGate.lean#L112) · [N2a clamp, Table 2 and Fig. S2](https://journals.plos.org/plosone/article?id=10.1371/journal.pone.0060506)

Search located representative wild-type recordings at multiple voltages in Revilla's Fig. 3A and the time constants and steady conductance across six pairs in Figs. 3B–C. At −100 mV the fast-to-slow amplitude ratio was 3. The published two-component fit leaves the third amplitude open. Their recovery trace in Fig. 1A returns to +20 mV. A separate study of wild-type and tagged Cx43 reported that the state of one gate affects the other, giving a biological reason to test the independence assumption. [Revilla et al., Figs. 1 and 3](https://doi.org/10.1016/S0006-3495(99)76986-9) · [Bukauskas et al. (2001)](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC1301499/)

The CPU comparison at engine `a69f38ab` covered 45 clamped samples and three tissue cell pairs. Maximum absolute errors against the independent reference were **1.11 × 10⁻¹⁶** in clamped gate fraction and **8.67 × 10⁻¹⁸ fmol** in final tracer transfer. Lean 4.19 checked the three new theorems. Six catalog tests, nine registry tests and 82 repository guard tests passed. The proposed two-gate law awaits implementation and its own numerical comparison. [Reproduce the run](README.md#cpu-comparisons) · [Claim for the registered gate](claims/junction-gate.md)
